template <class ExprSet>
SolutionData IDAKLUSolverOpenMP<ExprSet>::solve(
  const std::vector<sunrealtype> &t_eval,
  const std::vector<sunrealtype> &t_interp,
  const sunrealtype *y0,
  const sunrealtype *yp0,
  const sunrealtype *inputs,
  const sunrealtype *pbar,
  bool save_adaptive_steps,
  bool save_interp_steps
)
{
  DEBUG("IDAKLUSolver::solve");

  // Store solve parameters as member state
  save_adaptive_steps_ = save_adaptive_steps;
  save_interp_steps_ = save_interp_steps;

  const int number_of_evals = t_eval.size();

  // setup
  InitializeSolveStorage(number_of_evals, t_interp.size());
  SetupInitialState(t_eval, y0, yp0, inputs, pbar);

  sunrealtype t0 = t_eval.front();
  sunrealtype tf = t_eval.back();
  const bool increasing = (tf > t0);
  sunrealtype tf_perturbed = perturb_time(tf, increasing);

  sunrealtype t_val = t0;
  sunrealtype t_prev = t0;
  int i_eval = 1;
  sunrealtype t_eval_next = t_eval[i_eval];
  int i_interp = 0;
  sunrealtype t_interp_next = save_interp_steps_ ? t_interp[0] : 0;

  log_.log_start(t0, tf);

  // Check events on `yy` (= phi[0], the integrator's start state) before the
  // first step; a post-step IDAGetDky(t0) value can flip a boundary event's 
sign.
  event_values_.resize(number_of_events);
  rootsfound_.resize(number_of_events);
  if (number_of_events > 0) {
    events_eval<ExprSet>(t0, yy, yyp, event_values_.data(), functions.get());
    bool init_event_triggered = false;
    for (int i = 0; i < number_of_events; i++) {
      init_event_triggered |= (event_values_[i] <= 0.0);
    }
    if (init_event_triggered) {
      StoreInitialPoint(t0);
      log_.log_integration_complete(0, t0);
      return BuildSolutionData(IDA_ROOT_RETURN);
    }
  }

  // first step
  // Progress one step before the loop to ensure IDAGetDky works at t0 for dky =
1
  int n_steps = 0;
  int retval = IDASolve(ida_mem, tf_perturbed, &t_val, yy, yyp, IDA_ONE_STEP);
  GetSolutionFull(t0);

  log_.log_step(++n_steps, t_val);
  CheckErrors(retval, "IDASolve at t0");

  NoProgressGuard no_progression(solver_opts.num_steps_no_progress, 
solver_opts.t_no_progress);
  no_progression.Initialize();
  no_progression.AddDt(t_val - t0);

  StoreInitialPoint(t0);

  // Reset the states and sensitivities at t = t_val
  GetSolutionFull(t_val);

  // main integration loop
  DEBUG("IDASolve");
  while (true) {
    if (retval < 0) {
      break;
    } else if (t_prev == t_val || no_progression.Violated()) {
      retval = IDA_ERR_FAIL;
      break;
    }

    bool hit_teval = retval == IDA_TSTOP_RETURN;
    bool hit_final_time = t_val >= tf || (hit_teval && i_eval == 
number_of_evals);
    bool hit_event = retval == IDA_ROOT_RETURN;
    bool hit_adaptive = save_adaptive_steps_ && retval == IDA_SUCCESS;
    bool final_step = hit_final_time || hit_event;

    if (save_interp_steps_ && !use_knot_reduction_ && t_interp_next >= t_prev) {
      SaveInterpPoints(i_interp, t_interp_next, t_interp,
                       t_val, t_prev, t_eval_next);
    }

    if (hit_adaptive || hit_teval || hit_event || hit_final_time) {
      bool is_breakpoint = (hit_teval || hit_event) && !final_step;
      SavePoint(t_val, /*extend_arrays=*/hit_adaptive, is_breakpoint);
    }

    if (final_step) {
      break;
    } else if (hit_teval) {
      HandleBreakpoint(t_val, t_eval, i_eval, t_eval_next, no_progression);
    }

    t_prev = t_val;
    retval = IDASolve(ida_mem, tf_perturbed, &t_val, yy, yyp, IDA_ONE_STEP);
    GetSolutionStates(t_val);
    if (save_hermite) {
      GetSolutionDerivatives(t_val);
    }

    log_.log_step(++n_steps, t_val);
    no_progression.AddDt(t_val - t_prev);
  }

  log_.log_integration_complete(n_steps, t_val);

  return BuildSolutionData(retval);
}

template <class ExprSet>
void IDAKLUSolverOpenMP<ExprSet>::InitializeSolveStorage(
  int n_evals, int n_interps
) {
  DEBUG("IDAKLUSolver::InitializeSolveStorage");

  // Determine mode
  save_hermite = (
    solver_opts.hermite_interpolation &&
    save_adaptive_steps_ &&
    !save_outputs_only
  );

  use_knot_reduction_ = (
    solver_opts.hermite_reduction_factor > 1.0 &&
    save_hermite &&
    !sensitivity
  );

  length_of_return_vector = ReturnVectorLength();
  i_save_ = 0;

  // Allocate output arrays. Pre-allocate 64 elements for initial storage.
  int est = std::max(n_evals + n_interps, 64);

    def test_modulo_non_smooth_events(self):
        model = pybamm.BaseModel()
        var1 = pybamm.Variable("var1")
        var2 = pybamm.Variable("var2")

        a = 0.6
        discontinuities = (np.arange(3) + 1) * a

        model.rhs = {var1: pybamm.Modulo(pybamm.t, a)}
        model.algebraic = {var2: 2 * var1 - var2}
        model.initial_conditions = {var1: 0, var2: 0}
        model.events = [
            pybamm.Event("var1 = 0.55", pybamm.min(0.55 - var1)),
            pybamm.Event("var2 = 1.2", pybamm.min(1.2 - var2)),
        ]
        for discontinuity in discontinuities:
            model.events.append(
                pybamm.Event("nonsmooth rate", pybamm.Scalar(discontinuity))
            )
        disc = get_discretisation_for_testing()
        disc.process_model(model)

        step_solver = pybamm.CasadiSolver(rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-8)
        dt = 0.05
        time = 0
        end_time = 3
        step_solution = None
        while time < end_time:
            step_solution = step_solver.step(step_solution, model, dt=dt, 
npts=10)
            time += dt
        np.testing.assert_array_less(step_solution.y[0, :-1], 0.55)
        np.testing.assert_array_less(step_solution.y[-1, :-1], 1.2)
        np.testing.assert_equal(step_solution.t_event[0], step_solution.t[-1])
        np.testing.assert_array_equal(
            step_solution.y_event[:, 0], step_solution.y.full()[:, -1]
        )
        var1_soln = (step_solution.t % a) ** 2 / 2 + a**2 / 2 * (step_solution.t
// a)
        var2_soln = 2 * var1_soln
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            step_solution.y.full()[0], var1_soln, rtol=1e-5, atol=1e-4
        )
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            step_solution.y.full()[-1], var2_soln, rtol=1e-5, atol=1e-4
        )

    def test_step_restarts_sensitivities_from_output_variables(self):
        # Equal output and state widths, so seeding from the outputs is silently
wrong
        model = pybamm.BaseModel()
        u = pybamm.Variable("u")
        v = pybamm.Variable("v")
        model.rhs = {u: -pybamm.InputParameter("k") * u, v: u - 2 * v}
        model.initial_conditions = {u: 1.0, v: 0.5}
        model.variables = {"2u": 2 * u, "v": v}
        pybamm.Discretisation().process_model(model)
        solver = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(
            output_variables=["2u", "v"], rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-10
        )

        solution = solver.step(
            None, model, 1.0, inputs={"k": 1.3}, calculate_sensitivities=True
        )
        solution = solver.step(
            solution, model, 1.0, inputs={"k": 1.3}, 
calculate_sensitivities=True
        )

        # u = exp(-k t), so d(2u)/dk = -2 t exp(-k t) across both steps
        t = solution.t
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            solution["2u"].sensitivities["k"].ravel(),
            -2 * t * np.exp(-1.3 * t),
            rtol=1e-5,
            atol=1e-9,
        )

    def test_check_restart_sensitivities_allows_full_state(self):
        solution = pybamm.Solution(
            [np.array([0.0, 1.0])],
            [np.zeros((3, 2))],
            pybamm.BaseModel(),
            [{}],
            variables_returned=False,
        )
        solution._all_sensitivities = {"p": np.zeros((6, 1))}

        pybamm.BaseSolver._check_restart_sensitivities(solution)

    def test_check_restart_sensitivities_allows_empty_solution(self):
        pybamm.BaseSolver._check_restart_sensitivities(pybamm.EmptySolution())

def test_output_variables_sensitivities_to_some_inputs(self):
        model = pybamm.BaseModel()
        c = pybamm.Variable("c")
        a = pybamm.InputParameter("a")
        b = pybamm.InputParameter("b")
        model.rhs = {c: -a * c}
        model.initial_conditions = {c: 1}
        model.variables = {"w": b * c}
        t_interp = np.linspace(0, 1, 11)

        solver = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(output_variables=["w"], rtol=1e-8, 
atol=1e-10)
        sol = solver.solve(
            model,
            [0, 1],
            t_interp=t_interp,
            inputs={"a": 1.0, "b": 2.0},
            calculate_sensitivities=["b"],
        )

        # dw/db = c = e^-t; a is not a sensitivity input, so it has no entry
        sensitivities = sol["w"].sensitivities
        assert sorted(sensitivities) == ["all", "b"]
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            sensitivities["all"], np.exp(-t_interp)[:, np.newaxis], rtol=1e-6
        )
        np.testing.assert_allclose(sensitivities["b"], np.exp(-t_interp), 
rtol=1e-6)

    def test_output_variables_sensitivities_to_more_inputs_than_times(self):
        model = pybamm.BaseModel()
        u = pybamm.Variable("u")
        a, b, c = (pybamm.InputParameter(name) for name in "abc")
        model.rhs = {u: a + 2 * b + 3 * c}
        model.initial_conditions = {u: 0}
        model.variables = {"u": u}

        sol = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(output_variables=["u"]).solve(
            model,
            [0, 1],
            t_interp=np.array([0.0, 1.0]),
            inputs={"a": 1.0, "b": 1.0, "c": 1.0},
            calculate_sensitivities=True,
        )

        # u = (a + 2 b + 3 c) t
        for name, factor in zip("abc", [1, 2, 3], strict=True):
            np.testing.assert_allclose(
                sol["u"].sensitivities[name], [0, factor], atol=1e-10
            )
            np.testing.assert_allclose(
                sol.last_state.sensitivities[name], [[factor]], rtol=1e-10
            )

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("output_variables", [None, ["u"], ["u", "v"]])
    def test_step_with_output_variables_and_sensitivities(self, 
output_variables):
        model = pybamm.BaseModel()
        u = pybamm.Variable("u")
        v = pybamm.Variable("v")
        a = pybamm.InputParameter("a")
        model.rhs = {u: a * v, v: -v}
        model.initial_conditions = {u: 0, v: 1}
        model.variables = {"u": u, "v": v}
        solver = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(
            output_variables=output_variables, rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-10
        )

        sol = None
        for _ in range(2):
            sol = solver.step(
                sol, model, 1.0, inputs={"a": 2.0}, calculate_sensitivities=True
            )

        # du/da is the integral of v = e^-t, continued from the first step
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            sol["u"].sensitivities["a"], 1 - np.exp(-sol.t), rtol=1e-6, 
atol=1e-9
        )

    def test_with_output_variables_and_sensitivities(self):
        # Construct a model and solve for all variables, then test
        # the 'output_variables' option for each variable in turn, confirming
        # equivalence
        input_parameters = {  # Sensitivities dictionary
            "Current function [A]": 0.222,
            "Separator porosity": 0.3,
        }

        # construct model
        model = pybamm.lithium_ion.DFN()
        geometry = model.default_geometry
        param = model.default_parameter_values
        param.update({key: "[input]" for key in input_parameters})
        param.process_model(model)
        param.process_geometry(geometry)
        var_pts = {"x_n": 50, "x_s": 50, "x_p": 50, "r_n": 5, "r_p": 5}
        mesh = pybamm.Mesh(geometry, model.default_submesh_types, var_pts)
        disc = pybamm.Discretisation(mesh, model.default_spatial_methods)
        disc.process_model(model)

        t_eval = [0, 100]
        t_interp = np.linspace(t_eval[0], t_eval[-1], 5)

        options = {
            "linear_solver": "SUNLinSol_KLU",
            "jacobian": "sparse",
            "num_threads": 4,
            "max_num_steps": 1000,
        }

        # Use a selection of variables of different types
        output_variables = [
            "Voltage [V]",  # 0D
            "x [m]",  # 1D, empty sensitivities
            "Negative electrode potential [V]",  # 1D
            "Negative particle concentration [mol.m-3]",  # 2D
            "Throughput capacity [A.h]",  # ExplicitTimeIntegral
        ]

        # Use the full model as comparison (tested separately)
        solver_all = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(
            atol=1e-8,
            rtol=1e-8,
            options=options,
        )
        sol_all = solver_all.solve(
            model,
            t_eval,
            inputs=input_parameters,
            calculate_sensitivities=True,
            t_interp=t_interp,
        )

        # Solve for a subset of variables and compare results
        solver = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(
            atol=1e-8,
            rtol=1e-8,
            options=options,
            output_variables=output_variables,
        )
        sol = solver.solve(
            model,
            t_eval,
            inputs=input_parameters,
            calculate_sensitivities=True,
            t_interp=t_interp,
        )

        # Compare output to sol_all
        tol = 1e-5
        for varname in output_variables:
            np.testing.assert_allclose(
                sol[varname](t_interp),
                sol_all[varname](t_interp),
                rtol=tol,
                atol=tol,
            )

            # Test `all` key shape
            assert (
                sol[varname].sensitivities["all"].shape
                == sol_all[varname].sensitivities["all"].shape
            )

        # test each of the sensitivity calculations match
        for varname in output_variables:
            for key in input_parameters:
                np.testing.assert_allclose(
                    sol[varname].sensitivities[key],
                    sol_all[varname].sensitivities[key],
                    rtol=tol,
                    atol=tol,
                    err_msg=f"Failed for '{varname}', sensitivity '{key}'",
                )

    def test_output_variables_sensitivities_are_keyed_by_sensitivity_parameters(
        self,
    ):
        # An input that no sensitivity was requested for takes no key or column.
        parameter_values = pybamm.ParameterValues("Chen2020")
        inputs = {
            "Current function [A]": 0.68,
            "Electrode height [m]": parameter_values["Electrode height [m]"],
        }
        parameter_values.update({key: "[input]" for key in inputs})
        name = "Voltage [V]"
        t_interp = np.linspace(0, 600, 11)

        def voltage_sensitivities(output_variables):
            sim = pybamm.Simulation(
                pybamm.lithium_ion.SPM(),
                parameter_values=parameter_values,
                solver=pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(output_variables=output_variables),
            )
            solution = sim.solve(
                [0, 600],
                t_interp=t_interp,
                inputs=inputs,
                calculate_sensitivities=["Current function [A]"],
            )
            return solution[name].sensitivities

        full = voltage_sensitivities(None)
        outputs_only = voltage_sensitivities([name])
        assert set(outputs_only) == {"all", "Current function [A]"}
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            outputs_only["Current function [A]"],
            full["Current function [A]"],
            rtol=1e-6,
            atol=1e-10,
        )

    def test_with_output_variables_and_event_termination(self):

I'm going to write a reproduction script, r2.py, covering the step and 
sensitivity issues (3, 5, 6, 7), including the case where a RuntimeError leaves

$ cat > /tmp/repro/r2.py <<'EOF'
import numpy as np, pybamm
def run(name, f):
    try:
        print(name, "->", f())
    except Exception as e:
        print(name, "-> EXC", type(e).__name__, str(e)[:300])

def dae_model():
    m = pybamm.BaseModel()
    v = pybamm.Variable("v"); a = pybamm.Variable("a")
    ts = pybamm.InputParameter("t_switch")
    m.rhs = {v: a}
    m.algebraic = {a: a - 1 - 4 * (pybamm.t > ts)}
    m.initial_conditions = {v: 0, a: 1}
    m.variables = {"v": v, "a": a}
    return m
def f3():
    m = dae_model(); s = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver()
    sol = s.step(None, m, 10, inputs={"t_switch": 4.0})
    return sol["v"](10), "exp 34", sol.t
run("step dae jump", f3)
def f3mod():
    m = pybamm.BaseModel(); v = pybamm.Variable("v")
    m.rhs = {v: pybamm.Modulo(pybamm.t, 3.0)}
    m.initial_conditions = {v: 0}; m.variables = {"v": v}
    s = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver()
    sol = s.step(None, m, 10)
    sol = s.step(sol, m, 10)
    # integral of t mod 3 over 0..20: 6 full periods (4.5 each) + int_0^2 t = 2
    return sol["v"](20), "exp", 6*4.5+2, sol.t
run("step modulo", f3mod)
def f3modin():
    m = pybamm.BaseModel(); v = pybamm.Variable("v")
    m.rhs = {v: pybamm.Modulo(pybamm.t, pybamm.InputParameter("p"))}
    m.initial_conditions = {v: 0}; m.variables = {"v": v}
    s = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver()
    r = []
    for p, exp in [(3.0, 6*4.5+2), (4.0, 5*8), (np.inf, 200)]:
        sol = s.solve(m, [0, 20], inputs={"p": p})
        r.append((sol["v"](20), exp))
    return r
run("modulo input", f3modin)
def f5():
    m = pybamm.BaseModel(); u = pybamm.Variable("u")
    a = pybamm.InputParameter("a"); b = pybamm.InputParameter("b")
    m.rhs = {u: a + 2*b}; m.initial_conditions = {u: 0}; m.variables = {"u": u}
    s1 = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver()
    sol = s1.step(None, m, 1, inputs={"a": 1., "b": 1.}, 
calculate_sensitivities=["a"])
    s2 = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver()
    sol = s2.step(sol, m, 1, inputs={"a": 1., "b": 1.}, 
calculate_sensitivities=["b"])
    return sol["u"].sensitivities
run("a then b", f5)
def f6():
    m = pybamm.BaseModel(); u = pybamm.Variable("u")
    a = pybamm.InputParameter("a")
    m.rhs = {u: a}; m.initial_conditions = {u: 0}; m.variables = {"u": u}
    s = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver()
    sol = s.step(None, m, 1, inputs={"a": 1.}, calculate_sensitivities=True)
    sol = s.step(sol, m, 1, inputs={"a": 1.}, calculate_sensitivities=False)
    sol2 = s.step(None, m, 1, inputs={"a": 1.}, calculate_sensitivities=True)
    return sol["u"](2), sol2["u"].sensitivities["a"]
run("change sens step", f6)
def f6b():
    m = pybamm.BaseModel(); u = pybamm.Variable("u"); a = 
pybamm.InputParameter("a")
    m.rhs = {u: a}; m.initial_conditions = {u: 0}; m.variables = {"u": u}
    m2 = m.new_copy()
    s = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver()
    s.solve(m, [0, 1], inputs={"a": 1.})
    before = getattr(m2, "calculate_sensitivities", "unset")
    try:
        s.solve(m2, [0, 1], inputs={"a": 1.}, calculate_sensitivities=True)
    except RuntimeError as e:
        pass
    try:
        s.step(None, m2, 1, inputs={"a": 1.}, calculate_sensitivities=True)
    except RuntimeError as e:
        pass
    return before, m2.calculate_sensitivities
run("runtime error leaves", f6b)
def f7():
    m = pybamm.BaseModel(); u = pybamm.Variable("u"); ts = 
pybamm.InputParameter("ts")
    m.rhs = {u: pybamm.t > ts}; m.initial_conditions = {u: 0}; m.variables = 
{"u": u}
    s = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver()
    s.solve(m, [0, 10], inputs={"ts": 1.})
    n1 = len(m.events)
    s.solve(m, [0, 10], inputs={"ts": 1.}, calculate_sensitivities=True)
    s.solve(m, [0, 10], inputs={"ts": 1.}, calculate_sensitivities=False)
    return n1, len(m.events)
run("events dup", f7)
def f_sim():
    model = pybamm.lithium_ion.SPM()
    pv = model.default_parameter_values
    exp = pybamm.Experiment(["Discharge at 1C for 10 minutes", "Rest for 5 
minutes"])
    sim = pybamm.Simulation(model, experiment=exp, parameter_values=pv)
    sim.solve(inputs={}, calculate_sensitivities=False)
    return "ok"

import numpy as np, pybamm
def run(name, f):
    try:
        print(name, "->", f())
    except Exception as e:
        print(name, "-> EXC", type(e).__name__, str(e)[:300])
def dae_model():
    m = pybamm.BaseModel()
    v = pybamm.Variable("v"); a = pybamm.Variable("a")
    ts = pybamm.InputParameter("t_switch")
    m.rhs = {v: a}
    m.algebraic = {a: a - 1 - 4 * (pybamm.t > ts)}
    m.initial_conditions = {v: 0, a: 1}
    m.variables = {"v": v, "a": a}
    return m
def f3():
    m = dae_model(); s = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver()
    sol = s.step(None, m, 10, inputs={"t_switch": 4.0})
    return sol["v"](10), "exp 34", sol.t
run("step dae jump", f3)
def f3mod():
    m = pybamm.BaseModel(); v = pybamm.Variable("v")
    m.rhs = {v: pybamm.Modulo(pybamm.t, 3.0)}
    m.initial_conditions = {v: 0}; m.variables = {"v": v}
    s = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver()
    sol = s.step(None, m, 10)
    sol = s.step(sol, m, 10)
    # integral of t mod 3 over 0..20: 6 full periods (4.5 each) + int_0^2 t = 2
    return sol["v"](20), "exp", 6*4.5+2, sol.t
run("step modulo", f3mod)
def f3modin():
    m = pybamm.BaseModel(); v = pybamm.Variable("v")
    m.rhs = {v: pybamm.Modulo(pybamm.t, pybamm.InputParameter("p"))}
    m.initial_conditions = {v: 0}; m.variables = {"v": v}
    s = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver()
    r = []
    for p, exp in [(3.0, 6*4.5+2), (4.0, 5*8), (np.inf, 200)]:
        sol = s.solve(m, [0, 20], inputs={"p": p})
        r.append((sol["v"](20), exp))
    return r
run("modulo input", f3modin)
def f5():

    sol = s1.step(None, m, 1, inputs={"a": 1., "b": 1.}, 
calculate_sensitivities=["a"])
    s2 = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver()
    sol = s2.step(sol, m, 1, inputs={"a": 1., "b": 1.}, 
calculate_sensitivities=["b"])
    return sol["u"].sensitivities

    sol = s.step(sol, m, 1, inputs={"a": 1.}, calculate_sensitivities=False)

Reasoning:
I'm checking the case where "a then b" gives the combined solution the same 
sensitivities for both keys. With the fix in place, the solver should raise a 
SolverError naming 'b', since the earlier solve never produced sensitivities for
'b'. I also want to confirm the documented rule: a continued step() never reuses
sensitivities from another input.

I'm working through the events case where a list `t_eval` of [0, 10] fails with 
"at least 2 entries." I suspect `np.insert` turns the list into an integer 
array, so the inserted points collapse to [0, 0], which is the integer `t_eval` 
bug. The first solve passed only because its time points happened to be spaced 
differently. I'm also planning a sensitivity repro for issue 4 that compares 
against finite differences, using an input whose effect stays non-trivial after 
a model change.

I'm picking an experiment for the sensitivity repro: a discharge at 1C followed 
by a hold at 3.9 V, which should give different models for the current and 
voltage steps. Then I'd compare the sensitivity of a parameter like the positive
electrode diffusivity against central finite differences of the final voltage.



$ cat > /tmp/repro/r3.py <<'EOF'
import numpy as np, pybamm, sys
pybamm.set_logging_level("ERROR")
def make_sim(mode, name, extra=None):
    model = pybamm.lithium_ion.SPM()
    pv = pybamm.ParameterValues("Chen2020")
    pv.update({name: "[input]"})
    exp = pybamm.Experiment(["Discharge at 1C for 10 minutes", "Hold at 3.6 V 
for 5 minutes", "Discharge at 0.5C for 5 minutes"])
    return pybamm.Simulation(model, experiment=exp, parameter_values=pv, 
experiment_model_mode=mode)
def check(mode, name, val, rel=1e-3):
    try:
        sim = make_sim(mode, name)
        sol = sim.solve(inputs={name: val}, calculate_sensitivities=True)
        sens = sol["Voltage [V]"].sensitivities[name][-1].item()
        def V(p):
            s = make_sim(mode, name).solve(inputs={name: p})
            return s["Voltage [V]"].entries[-1]
        h = val * rel
        fd = (V(val + h) - V(val - h)) / (2 * h)
        print(mode, name, "sens", sens, "fd", fd)
    except Exception as e:
        import traceback; traceback.print_exc()
        print(mode, name, "EXC", type(e).__name__, e)
for mode in sys.argv[1:] or ["legacy", "unified"]:
    check(mode, "Positive particle diffusivity [m2.s-1]", 4e-15)
    check(mode, "Maximum concentration in negative electrode [mol.m-3]", 
33133.0)

$ cat > /tmp/repro/r4.py <<'EOF'
import numpy as np, pybamm, sys
pybamm.set_logging_level("ERROR")
name = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "Positive particle diffusivity 
[m2.s-1]"
val = float(sys.argv[3]) if len(sys.argv) > 3 else 4e-15
mode = sys.argv[1]
steps_list = [
    ["Discharge at 1C for 10 minutes"],
    ["Discharge at 1C for 10 minutes", "Discharge at 0.5C for 5 minutes"],
    ["Discharge at 1C for 10 minutes", "Rest for 5 minutes"],
    ["Discharge at 1C for 10 minutes", "Hold at 3.6 V for 5 minutes"],
]
for steps in steps_list:
    def sim():
        model = pybamm.lithium_ion.SPM()
        pv = pybamm.ParameterValues("Chen2020")
        pv.update({name: "[input]"})
        return pybamm.Simulation(model, experiment=pybamm.Experiment(steps), 
parameter_values=pv, experiment_model_mode=mode,
                                 solver=pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(rtol=1e-8, 
atol=1e-10))
    try:
        sol = sim().solve(inputs={name: val}, calculate_sensitivities=True)
        sens = sol["Voltage [V]"].sensitivities[name][-1].item()
        V = lambda p: sim().solve(inputs={name: p})["Voltage [V]"].entries[-1]
        h = val * 1e-4
        fd = (V(val + h) - V(val - h)) / (2 * h)
        print(steps, "sens", sens, "fd", fd, "ratio", sens / fd)
    except Exception as e:
        print(steps, "EXC", type(e).__name__, e)
EOF

Tool: terminal
Result:
return self._entries_raw is not None

    @property
    def xr_array_raw_initialized(self):
        return self._xr_array_raw is not None

    @property
    def sensitivities(self):
        """
        Returns a dictionary of sensitivities for each input parameter.
        The keys are the input parameters, and the value is a matrix of size
        (n_x * n_t, n_p), where n_x is the number of states, n_t is the number 
of time
        points, and n_p is the size of the input parameter
        """
        # No sensitivities if there are no inputs
        if len(self.all_inputs[0]) == 0:
            return {}
        # Otherwise initialise and return sensitivities
        if self._sensitivities is None:
            if self.all_solution_sensitivities:
                self.initialise_sensitivity_explicit_forward()
            else:
                raise ValueError(
                    "Cannot compute sensitivities. The 'calculate_sensitivities'
"
                    "argument of the solver.solve should be changed from 'None' 
to "
                    "allow sensitivities calculations. Check solver 
documentation for "
                    "details."
                )
        return self._sensitivities

    def initialise_sensitivity_explicit_forward(self):
        "Set up the sensitivity dictionary"
        self._sensitivities = self._observer.sensitivities(self)

    def _is_discrete_time_method(self):
        """Check if using discrete time integral method"""
        return (
            self.time_integral is not None and self.time_integral.method == 
"discrete"
        )

    @property
    def hermite_interpolation(self):
        return self.all_yps is not None

    def as_computed(self):
        """
        Allows a ProcessedVariable to be converted to a 
ComputedProcessedVariable for
        use together, e.g. when using a last state solution with a new 
simulation running
        with output variables in the solver.
        """
        sensitivities = self._sensitivities
        if sensitivities is None and self.all_solution_sensitivities:
            sensitivities = self.sensitivities

        def _stub_solution(self):
            """
            Return a lightweight object that looks like the parts of
            `pybamm.Solution` required by ProcessedVariableComputed, but without
            keeping the full state vector in memory.
            """

            class StubSolution:
                def __init__(
                    self, ts, ys, inputs, inputs_stacked, sensitivities, t_pts
                ):
                    self.all_ts = ts
                    self.all_ys = ys
                    self.all_inputs = inputs
                    self.all_inputs_stacked = inputs_stacked
                    self.sensitivities = sensitivities
                    self.t = t_pts

            return StubSolution(
                self.all_ts,
                self.all_ys,
                self.all_inputs,
                self.all_inputs_stacked,
                sensitivities or {},
                self.t_pts,
            )

        if self.time_integral is None:
            # Rows as an output_variables solve returns them: before per-class 
reordering
            observed = self._observe_raw()
            base_data = [observed.reshape(-1, len(self.t_pts), order="F").T]
        elif isinstance(self, ProcessedVariable0D):
            # output_variables stores a time integral as its single summed value
            base_data = [self.entries]
        else:
            raise NotImplementedError(
                f"Variable {self._name!r}: as_computed() does not support time "
                "integrals of spatially varying variables."
            )

        cpv = pybamm.ProcessedVariableComputed(
            self.base_variables,
            self._observer.leaves,
            base_data,
            _stub_solution(self),
            time_integral=self.time_integral,
        )

        # add sensitivities if they exist
        if sensitivities:
            cpv._sensitivities = sensitivities

        return cpv

    @_deprecated_leaves_keyword
    def __init__(
        self,
        name: str,
        base_variables,
        observer,
        solution,
        time_integral: pybamm.ProcessedVariableTimeIntegral | None = None,
    ):
        self.dimensions = 0
        super().__init__(

Next I'm working on IDAKLU's consistent algebraic sensitivities for restarts, by
adding a helper that runs only when sensitivities are on, the restart flag is 
set, and the model has algebraic variables.

Now I'm computing the algebraic sensitivities for the restart case by building 
the Jacobian blocks of the algebraic equations with respect to the differential 
and algebraic states, plus the parameter Jacobian, and solving for the algebraic
sensitivities.

Checking the casadi Jacobian route for the restart case: the algebraic Jacobian 
block with respect to the algebraic states is the one to invert, and the 
parameter Jacobian per input name is already available on the model.

Now I'm checking whether the IDAS consistent initialisation also computes 
consistent sensitivities when calc_ic is on at a restart. I think the IDACalcIC 
routine handles sensitivities when they are enabled, so the algebraic 
sensitivities may already be covered there.

`_set_sens_initial_conditions_from` name and adding an optional `inputs`

  old_str: 
        def _set_up_events(self, model, t_eval, inputs: list[dict], 
vars_for_processing):
            # Check for heaviside and modulo functions in rhs and algebraic and 
add
            # discontinuity events if these exist.
            # Note: only checks for the case of t < X, t <= X, X < t, or X <= t,
            # but also accounts for the fact that t might be dimensional
            tf = np.max(t_eval)
    
            def supports_t_eval_discontinuities(expr):
                # Only IDAKLUSolver supports discontinuities represented by 
t_eval
                return self.supports_t_eval_discontinuities and 
expr.is_constant()
    
            # Find all the constant time-based discontinuities
            t_discon_symbols = []
    
            for symbol in itertools.chain(
                model.concatenated_rhs.pre_order(),
                model.concatenated_algebraic.pre_order(),
            ):
                if isinstance(symbol, _Heaviside):
                    if symbol.right == pybamm.t:
                        expr = symbol.left
                    elif symbol.left == pybamm.t:
                        expr = symbol.right
                    else:
                        # Heaviside function does not contain pybamm.t as an 
argument.
                        # Do not create an event
                        continue  # pragma: no cover
    
                    if supports_t_eval_discontinuities(expr):
                        # save the symbol and expression ready for evaluation 
later
                        t_discon_symbols.append((symbol, expr, None))
                    else:
                        model.events.append(
                            pybamm.Event(
                                str(symbol),
                                expr,
                                pybamm.EventType.DISCONTINUITY,
                            )
                        )
    
                elif isinstance(symbol, pybamm.Modulo) and symbol.left == 
pybamm.t:
                    expr = symbol.right
                    num_events = 200 if (t_eval is None) else (tf // expr.value)
    
                    if supports_t_eval_discontinuities(expr):
                        t_discon_symbols.append((symbol, expr, num_events))
                    else:
                        for i in np.arange(num_events):
                            model.events.append(
                                pybamm.Event(
                                    str(symbol),
                                    expr * pybamm.Scalar(i + 1),
                                    pybamm.EventType.DISCONTINUITY,
                                )
                            )
                else:
                    continue

  new_str: 
        def _set_up_events(self, model, t_eval, inputs: list[dict], 
vars_for_processing):
            # Check for heaviside and modulo functions in rhs and algebraic and 
add
            # discontinuity events if these exist.
            # Note: only checks for the case of t < X, t <= X, X < t, or X <= t,
            # but also accounts for the fact that t might be dimensional
    
            # Time-constant discontinuities, as (symbol, expression, 
num_events).
            # Modulo resets are always listed here, and are timed from the 
window
            # being integrated (see _get_discontinuity_start_end_indices).
            t_discon_symbols = []
    
            for symbol in itertools.chain(
                model.concatenated_rhs.pre_order(),
                model.concatenated_algebraic.pre_order(),
            ):
                if isinstance(symbol, _Heaviside):
                    if symbol.right == pybamm.t:
                        expr = symbol.left
                    elif symbol.left == pybamm.t:
                        expr = symbol.right
                    else:
                        # Heaviside function does not contain pybamm.t as an 
argument.
                        # Do not create an event
                        continue  # pragma: no cover
    
                    if self.supports_t_eval_discontinuities and 
expr.is_constant():
                        # save the symbol and expression ready for evaluation 
later
                        t_discon_symbols.append((symbol, expr, None))
                    elif not any(
                        event.event_type == pybamm.EventType.DISCONTINUITY
                        and event.name == str(symbol)
                        and event.expression == expr
                        for event in model.events
                    ):
                        model.events.append(
                            pybamm.Event(
                                str(symbol),
                                expr,
                                pybamm.EventType.DISCONTINUITY,
                            )
                        )
    
                elif isinstance(symbol, pybamm.Modulo) and symbol.left == 
pybamm.t:
                    expr = symbol.right
                    if expr.has_symbol_of_classes(
                        (
                            pybamm.Time,
                            pybamm.StateVector,
                            pybamm.StateVectorDot,
                            pybamm.Variable,
                        )
                    ):
                        raise pybamm.SolverError(
                            f"The period of '{symbol}' must not depend on time 
or on "
                            "the model states, but it is time-dependent: "
                            f"'{expr}'"
                        )
                    t_discon_symbols.append((symbol, expr, None))

\"\"\"Split ``t_eval`` into the segments to integrate between 
discontinuities.\n\n        Time-constant discontinuities that the solver 
handles as breakpoints in\n        ``t_eval`` are inserted into it; every other

# after it. One within a float of an end of t_eval, or of the previous\n        
# discontinuity, leaves no room for a segment, so it is not split on.\n

+ eps))\n            left = min(*candidates, np.nextafter(dtime, -np.inf))\n

t_eval[-1]:\n                continue\n            lefts.append(left)\n

  old_str: 
        def _get_discontinuity_start_end_indices(self, model, y0, inputs, 
t_eval):
            if self.supports_t_eval_discontinuities and 
model.t_discon_constant_symbols:
                pybamm.logger.verbose("Discontinuity events found for constant 
symbols")
                _t_discon_constant = []
                for symbol, expr, num_events in model.t_discon_constant_symbols:
                    _t_discon_constant.extend(
                        symbol._t_discon(expr, y0, inputs, num_events)
                    )
    
                t_discon_constant = 
self.filter_discontinuities(_t_discon_constant, t_eval)
                t_eval = np.union1d(t_eval, t_discon_constant)
    
            if not model.discontinuity_events_eval:
                pybamm.logger.verbose("No additional discontinuity events 
found")
                return [0], [len(t_eval)], t_eval
    
            # Calculate all possible discontinuities
            _t_discon_full = [
                event.expression.evaluate(inputs=inputs)
                for event in model.discontinuity_events_eval
            ]
            t_discon = self.filter_discontinuities(_t_discon_full, t_eval)
    
            pybamm.logger.verbose(f"Discontinuity events found at t = 
{t_discon}")
    
            # insert time points around discontinuities in t_eval
            # keep track of subsections to integrate by storing start and end 
indices
            start_indices = [0]
            end_indices = []
            eps = sys.float_info.epsilon
            for dtime in t_discon:
                dindex = np.searchsorted(t_eval, dtime, side="left")
                end_indices.append(dindex + 1)
                start_indices.append(dindex + 1)
                if dtime * (1 - eps) < t_eval[dindex] < dtime * (1 + eps):
                    t_eval[dindex] *= 1 + eps
                    t_eval = np.insert(t_eval, dindex, dtime * (1 - eps))
                else:
                    t_eval = np.insert(
                        t_eval, dindex, [dtime * (1 - eps), dtime * (1 + eps)]
                    )
            end_indices.append(len(t_eval))
    
            return start_indices, end_indices, t_eval

  new_str: 
        @staticmethod
        def _modulo_reset_times(expr, y0, inputs, t_eval) -> np.ndarray:
            """Times in ``[t_eval[0], t_eval[-1]]`` at which ``t mod expr`` 
resets.
    
            Parameters
            ----------
            expr : :class:`pybamm.Symbol`
                The period, which may depend on input parameters.
            y0 : array-like
                The state at ``t_eval[0]``.
            inputs : dict
                Input parameters for one input set.
            t_eval : :class:`numpy.ndarray`
                The integration window.
    
            Returns
            -------
            :class:`numpy.ndarray`
                The reset times, empty for an infinite or zero period.
            """
            period = np.abs(np.asarray(expr.evaluate(0, y0, inputs=inputs), 
dtype=float))
            if period.size != 1:
                raise pybamm.SolverError(
                    f"The period of a Modulo must be a scalar, not of shape 
{period.shape}"
                )
            period = period.item()
            if not np.isfinite(period) or period == 0:
                return np.empty(0)
            first = np.floor(t_eval[0] / period)
            last = np.floor(t_eval[-1] / period) + 1
            return np.arange(first, last + 1) * period
    
        def _get_discontinuity_start_end_indices(self, model, y0, inputs, 
t_eval):
            """Split ``t_eval`` into the segments to integrate between 
discontinuities.
    
            Time-constant discontinuities that the solver handles as breakpoints
in
            ``t_eval`` are inserted into it; every other discontinuity in the 
window
            ends one segment just before it and starts the next just after it.
    
            Parameters
            ----------
            model : :class:`pybamm.BaseModel`
                The set-up model.
            y0 : array-like
                The state at ``t_eval[0]``.
            inputs : dict
                Input parameters for one input set.
            t_eval : array-like
                The integration times, which are not modified.
    
            Returns
            -------
            start_indices : list of int
                Index in the returned ``t_eval`` of the first time of each 
segment.
            end_indices : list of int
                Index in the returned ``t_eval`` just after the last time of 
each
                segment.
            t_eval : :class:`numpy.ndarray`
                The integration times, as floats, with the discontinuity times 
added.
            """
            t_eval = np.array(t_eval, dtype=float).reshape(-1)
    
            breakpoints = []
            split_times = []
            for symbol, expr, num_events in model.t_discon_constant_symbols:
                as_breakpoints = self.supports_t_eval_discontinuities and 
expr.is_constant()
                if isinstance(symbol, pybamm.Modulo):
                    times = self._modulo_reset_times(expr, y0, inputs, t_eval)
                    if as_breakpoints:
                        # Stop right before each reset and at it
                        breakpoints.extend(np.nextafter(times, -np.inf))
                        breakpoints.extend(times)
                    else:
                        split_times.extend(times)
                else:
                    breakpoints.extend(symbol._t_discon(expr, y0, inputs, 
num_events))
    
            if breakpoints:
                pybamm.logger.verbose("Discontinuity events found for constant 
symbols")
                t_eval = np.union1d(
                    t_eval, self.filter_discontinuities(breakpoints, t_eval)
                )
    
            split_times.extend(
                np.ravel(event.expression.evaluate(inputs=inputs))
                for event in model.discontinuity_events_eval
            )
            if not split_times:
                pybamm.logger.verbose("No additional discontinuity events 
found")
                return [0], [len(t_eval)], t_eval
    
            t_discon = self.filter_discontinuities(np.hstack(split_times), 
t_eval)
            pybamm.logger.verbose(f"Discontinuity events found at t = 
{t_discon}")
    
            # Each segment ends just before a discontinuity and the next starts 
just
            # after it. One within a float of an end of t_eval, or of the 
previous
            # discontinuity, leaves no room for a segment, so it is not split 
on.
            eps = sys.float_info.epsilon
            lefts = []
            rights = []
            for dtime in t_discon:
                candidates = (dtime * (1 - eps), dtime * (1 + eps))
                left = min(*candidates, np.nextafter(dtime, -np.inf))
                right = max(*candidates, np.nextafter(dtime, np.inf))
                previous = rights[-1] if rights else t_eval[0]
                if left <= previous or right >= t_eval[-1]:
                    continue
                lefts.append(left)
                rights.append(right)
    
            if not lefts:
                return [0], [len(t_eval)], t_eval
    
            # Drop the requested times within a float of a discontinuity
            lefts = np.array(lefts)
            rights = np.array(rights)
            index = np.searchsorted(rights, t_eval, side="left")
            inside = (index < len(lefts)) & (
                t_eval >= lefts[np.minimum(index, len(lefts) - 1)]
            )
            t_eval = np.sort(np.concatenate([t_eval[~inside], lefts, rights]))
    
            start_indices = [0, *np.searchsorted(t_eval, rights).tolist()]
            end_indices = [*(np.searchsorted(t_eval, lefts) + 1).tolist(), 
len(t_eval)]
            return start_indices, end_indices, t_eval

Reasoning:
I'm checking whether np.hstack can take a mix of plain floats and 1D arrays, and
it can, since it promotes each item with atleast_1d first. I also need to keep 
in mind that the modulo split times can fall outside the range, which the filter
step already handles. For the inside mask, I'm finding the first interval whose 
right edge is at or past each evaluation point and checking it against the left 
edge, which works because the intervals don't overlap.

I'm checking whether `model.t_discon_constant_symbols` might be missing on some 
models, since the original code only reached it when the solver supports 
discontinuities, so a BaseSolver mock could break. I'm thinking about using 
`getattr` with a default of an empty list to make it safe.

I'm reasoning through the Modulo split times: with a constant period, the reset 
points run up to the end time, and I want to confirm the boundary filter 
excludes the right values at the endpoint.

Next I'm revisiting how the time-dependent reset logic diverges from the 
original: the old code fixed the event count at setup, so a later solve with a 
longer t_eval used stale values, whereas mine computes them dynamically. Then 
I'll rewrite the solve() loop to process discontinuities and add an 
`_integrate_segments` helper, starting from the "Process discontinuities" 
section.

                for sol in new_solutions:
                    sol.solve_time = solve_time
                if start_index == start_indices[0]:
                    solutions = [sol for sol in new_solutions]
                else:
                    for i, new_solution in enumerate(new_solutions):
                        solutions[i] = solutions[i] + new_solution
    
                if solutions[0].termination != "final time":
                    break
    
                if end_index != len(t_eval):
                    # setup for next integration subsection
                    for i, soln in enumerate(new_solutions):
                        last_state = soln.y[:, -1]
                        # update y0 (for DAE solvers, this updates the initial 
guess for the
                        # rootfinder)
                        model.y0_list[i] = last_state
                    if len(model.algebraic) > 0:
                        model.y0_list = self.calculate_consistent_state(
                            model, t_eval[end_index], model_inputs_list
                        )
            solve_time = timer.time()

  new_str:

states

Reasoning:
I'm wondering whether setting attributes to None for ones that didn't exist, 
like y0full on non-IDAKLU models, is a problem. Since that adds attributes 
nobody needs, it's cleaner to only restore the ones that actually exist, so I'll
filter with hasattr when building the initial_states dict.

Restoring y0_list after solve looks like a no-op in the original, since the 
solver mutates it in place and leaves the last segment's state behind. Restoring
the true initial values is what the original code intended, and a single segment
with no discontinuities behaves the same as before.

Reasoning:
I'm checking that the `termination` value returned after the loop is fine, since
it only feeds the logging. Next I'll add the four helper methods, placing them 
right after `solve` and before `filter_discontinuities`.



Thought:
Now add the segment-integration helpers after `solve()`:

Action: FileEditorAction

Arguments:
  command: "str_replace"
  path: "/app/pybamm/packages/pybamm/src/pybamm/solvers/base_solver.py"
  old_str: 
        @staticmethod
        def filter_discontinuities(t_discon: list, t_eval: list) -> np.ndarray:

  new_str: 
        def _integrate_segments(
            self,
            model,
            t_eval,
            start_indices,
            end_indices,
            inputs_list,
            t_interp,
            nproc,
            timer,
        ):
            """Integrate each input set across the segments between 
discontinuities.
    
            Each input set restarts every segment from the state and 
sensitivities
            it reached at the end of the previous one, and stops at the first
            segment it does not integrate to the end of.
    
            Parameters
            ----------
            model : :class:`pybamm.BaseModel`
                The set-up model, initialised for every input set.
            t_eval : :class:`numpy.ndarray`
                The integration times of all the segments.
            start_indices, end_indices : list of int
                The bounds of each segment in ``t_eval``.
            inputs_list : list of dict
                Input parameters for each input set.
            t_interp : :class:`numpy.ndarray`
                The interpolation times, possibly empty.
            nproc : int or None
                Number of processes to integrate the input sets with.
            timer : :class:`pybamm.Timer`
                The timer that the segments' solve times are read from.
    
            Returns
            -------
            list of :class:`pybamm.Solution`
                One solution per input set.
            """
            solutions = [None] * len(inputs_list)
            active = list(range(len(inputs_list)))
            new_solutions = []
            for start_index, end_index in zip(start_indices, end_indices, 
strict=True):
                t_segment = t_eval[start_index:end_index]
                active_inputs = [inputs_list[i] for i in active]
                if start_index != start_indices[0]:
                    self._restart_from_solutions(
                        model, t_segment[0], new_solutions, active_inputs
                    )
                pybamm.logger.verbose(
                    f"Calling solver for {t_segment[0]} < t < {t_segment[-1]}"
                )
                new_solutions = self._integrate(
                    model,
                    t_segment,
                    active_inputs,
                    self._segment_t_interp(t_interp, t_segment),
                    nproc=nproc,
                )
                # pybamm.Solution.__add__ assumes attribute solve_time.
                solve_time = timer.time()
                for i, new_solution in zip(active, new_solutions, strict=True):
                    new_solution.solve_time = solve_time
                    if solutions[i] is None:
                        solutions[i] = new_solution
                    else:
                        solutions[i] = solutions[i] + new_solution
    
                still_active = [
                    k
                    for k, i in enumerate(active)
                    if solutions[i].termination == "final time"
                ]
                active = [active[k] for k in still_active]
                new_solutions = [new_solutions[k] for k in still_active]
                if not active:
                    break
            return solutions
    
        @staticmethod
        def _segment_t_interp(t_interp, t_segment):
            """The interpolation times within one segment.
    
            A segment without any keeps its own end points, since an empty
            ``t_interp`` asks the solver for every step instead.
            """
            if len(t_interp) == 0:
                return t_interp
            t_interp = np.asarray(t_interp, dtype=float)
            in_segment = t_interp[(t_interp >= t_segment[0]) & (t_interp <= 
t_segment[-1])]
            if in_segment.size == 0:
                return np.array([t_segment[0], t_segment[-1]])
            return in_segment
    
        def _restart_from_solutions(self, model, time, solutions, inputs_list):
            """Initialise ``model`` from the final states of ``solutions``.
    
            Parameters
            ----------
            model : :class:`pybamm.BaseModel`
                The set-up model.
            time : float
                The time to restart at.
            solutions : list of :class:`pybamm.Solution`
                The solution each input set restarts from.
            inputs_list : list of dict
                Input parameters for each input set.
            """
            names = model.calculate_sensitivities
            y0_list = []
            y0S_list = []
            for solution in solutions:
                last_state = solution.last_state
                y0 = last_state.all_ys[0]
                if isinstance(y0, casadi.DM):
                    y0 = y0.full()
                y0_list.append(np.asarray(y0)[:, -1])
                if names and model.y0S_list is not None:
                    y0S_list.append(self._state_sensitivities_by_name(last_state
, names))
            model.y0_list = y0_list
            if y0S_list:
                model.y0S_list = y0S_list
            self._set_consistent_initialization(model, time, inputs_list, 
restart=True)
    
        @staticmethod
        def _state_sensitivities_by_name(last_state, names) -> tuple:
            """The final state sensitivities of a solution to each input in 
``names``.
    
            Parameters
            ----------
            last_state : :class:`pybamm.Solution`
                The final state of a solution.
            names : list of str
                The inputs to differentiate with respect to.
    
            Returns
            -------
            tuple of :class:`numpy.ndarray`
                One flat state sensitivity per name.
    
            Raises
            ------
            :class:`pybamm.SolverError`
                If the solution has no sensitivities to some of ``names``.
            """
            sensitivities = last_state._all_sensitivities
            missing = [name for name in names if name not in sensitivities]
            if missing:
                raise pybamm.SolverError(
                    "Cannot continue a sensitivity solve from a solution that 
has no "
                    f"sensitivities to the inputs {missing}. Solve it with these
inputs "
                    "in 'calculate_sensitivities', or start a new solve."
                )
            n_states = last_state.all_models[-1].len_rhs_and_alg
            y0S = []
            for name in names:
                value = sensitivities[name]
                if isinstance(value, list):
                    value = value[-1]
                y0S.append(np.asarray(value, 
dtype=float).reshape(-1)[-n_states:])
            return tuple(y0S)
    
        @staticmethod
        def filter_discontinuities(t_discon: list, t_eval: list) -> np.ndarray:

  kind: "FileEditorAction"

+1157|                    model, t_segment[0], new_solutions, active_inputs

+1167|                nproc=nproc,
+1168|            )

+1170|            solve_time = timer.time()

strict=True):

+1185|            if not active:

+1189|    @staticmethod
+1190|    def _segment_t_interp(t_interp, t_segment):
+1191|        """The interpolation times within one segment.
+1192|
+1193|        A segment without any keeps its own end points, since an empty
+1194|        ``t_interp`` asks the solver for every step instead.
+1195|        """
+1196|        if len(t_interp) == 0:
+1197|            return t_interp
+1198|        t_interp = np.asarray(t_interp, dtype=float)
+1199|        in_segment = t_interp[(t_interp >= t_segment[0]) & (t_interp <= 
t_segment[-1])]
+1200|        if in_segment.size == 0:
+1201|            return np.array([t_segment[0], t_segment[-1]])
+1202|        return in_segment
+1203|
+1204|    def _restart_from_solutions(self, model, time, solutions, 
inputs_list):

+1206|
+1207|        Parameters

+1211|        time : float
+1212|            The time to restart at.
+1213|        solutions : list of :class:`pybamm.Solution`
+1214|            The solution each input set restarts from.
+1215|        inputs_list : list of dict

+1217|        """
+1218|        names = model.calculate_sensitivities
+1219|        y0_list = []

restart=True)

+1234|    @staticmethod

+1236|        """The final state sensitivities of a solution to each input in

+1237|
+1238|        Parameters
+1239|        ----------
+1240|        last_state : :class:`pybamm.Solution`
+1241|            The final state of a solution.
+1242|        names : list of str
+1243|            The inputs to differentiate with respect to.
+1244|
+1245|        Returns
+1246|        -------
+1247|        tuple of :class:`numpy.ndarray`

+1249|

+1253|            If the solution has no sensitivities to some of ``names``.

+1256|        missing = [name for name in names if name not in sensitivities]

    def _integrate_single(self, model, t_eval, inputs_dict, y0):
        """
        Solve a single DAE model defined by residuals with initial conditions 
y0.

        Parameters
        ----------
        model : :class:`pybamm.BaseModel`
            The model whose solution to calculate.
        t_eval : numeric type
            The times at which to compute the solution
        inputs_dict : dict, optional
            Any input parameters to pass to the model when solving
        y0 : array-like
            The initial conditions for the model

        Returns
        -------
        :class:`pybamm.Solution`
            A Solution object containing the times and values of the solution,
            as well as various diagnostic messages.
        """

        # casadi solver does not support sensitivity analysis
        if model.calculate_sensitivities:
            raise NotImplementedError(
                "Sensitivity analysis is not implemented for the CasADi solver."
            )

        # Record whether there are any symbolic inputs
        inputs_dict = inputs_dict or {}

        # convert inputs to casadi format
        inputs = casadi.vertcat(*[x for x in inputs_dict.values()])

        if self.mode in ["fast", "fast with events"] or not model.events:
            if not model.events:
                pybamm.logger.info("No events found, running fast mode")
            if self.mode == "fast with events":
                # Create the integrator with an event switch that will set the 
rhs to
                # zero when voltage limits are crossed
                use_event_switch = True
            else:
                use_event_switch = False
            # Create an integrator with the grid (we just need to do this once)
            self.create_integrator(
                model, y0, inputs, t_eval, use_event_switch=use_event_switch

    def _set_consistent_initialization(
        self, model: pybamm.BaseModel, time: float, inputs_list: list[dict]
    ):
        """
        Set initialized states for the model. This is skipped if the solver is 
an
        algebraic solver (since this would make the algebraic solver redundant),
and if
        the model doesn't have any algebraic equations (since there are no 
initial
        conditions to be calculated in this case).

        Parameters
        ----------
        model : :class:`pybamm.BaseModel`
            The model for which to calculate initial conditions.
        time : numeric type
            The time at which to calculate the initial conditions
        inputs_list : list of dict
            Any input parameters to pass to the model when solving
        """

        if self._algebraic_solver or model.len_alg == 0:
            # Don't update model.y0_list
            return

        # Calculate consistent states for the algebraic equations
        model.y0_list = self.calculate_consistent_state(model, time, 
inputs_list)

set and there's no other model to fall back on.

and I'm planning to make the sensitivities argument helper pure so it only 
computes the list and a changed flag. If they've changed, the model gets popped,
and then if it isn't in the set yet I'll assign the new sensitivities and call 
set_up.

                concatenated_initial_conditions.evaluate(0, inputs=model_inputs)

            if sensitivity_names:

                        old_solution, model, model_inputs

outputs are the derivatives with respect to time, the states and the parameters,
and if there are no parameters the parameter block simply has zero columns.

point.

inputs and the old solution's inputs could hold different values for the same 
name.

its processed value and sensitivities, and I need the derivatives of its 
reference and scale with respect to the inputs, so I'm sketching a helper that 
turns the inputs into symbols and evaluates the expression.

I'm sketching a fallback helper that turns the inputs into symbols and takes 
jacobians of the expression, though it's getting long. Before writing it I want 
to know when the mapper could fail where the value path still works, since that 
decides whether the fallback is needed at all.

I'm settling on dropping the fallback path: if the mapper can't be built, the 
error should propagate rather than quietly giving wrong sensitivities. The 
fallback would rarely be needed anyway, since the mapper only fails in cases 
where the value path would hit a shape mismatch too.

Now I'm going back over whether the mapper could fail for the last model in the 
old solution, since the state values should match that model. If it does fail, 
the error should just propagate rather than falling back to anything.

    def _set_sens_initial_conditions_from(
        self, solution: pybamm.Solution, model: pybamm.BaseModel
    ) -> tuple:
        """
        A restricted version of BaseModel.set_initial_conditions_from that only 
extracts the
        sensitivities from a solution object, and only for a model that has been
discretised.
        This is used when setting the initial conditions for a sensitivity 
model.

        Parameters
        ----------
        solution : :class:`pybamm.Solution`
            The solution to use to initialize the model

        model: :class:`pybamm.BaseModel`
            The model whose sensitivities to set

        Returns
        -------

        initial_conditions : tuple of ndarray
            The initial conditions for the sensitivities, each element of the 
tuple
            corresponds to an input parameter
        """

        ninputs = len(model.calculate_sensitivities)
        initial_conditions = tuple([] for _ in range(ninputs))
        solution = solution.last_state
        for var in model.initial_conditions:
            final_state = solution[var.name]
            final_state = final_state.sensitivities
            final_state_eval = tuple(
                final_state[key] for key in model.calculate_sensitivities
            )

            scale, reference = var.scale.value, var.reference.value
            for i in range(ninputs):
                scaled_final_state_eval = (final_state_eval[i] - reference) / 
scale
                initial_conditions[i].append(scaled_final_state_eval)

        # Also update the concatenated initial conditions if the model is 
already
        # discretised
        # Unpack slices for sorting
        y_slices = {var: slce for var, slce in model.y_slices.items()}
        slices = [y_slices[symbol][0] for symbol in model.initial_conditions]

        # sort equations according to slices
        concatenated_initial_conditions = [
            casadi.vertcat(*[eq for _, eq in sorted(zip(slices, init, 
strict=True))])
            for init in initial_conditions
        ]
        return concatenated_initial_conditions

    def _store_first_last_endpoints(self, t_eval):
        if not self.store_first_last:
            return None
        if not self.supports_interp:
            if not self._warned_store_first_last_no_op:
                warnings.warn(
                    f"store_first_last has no effect on {self.name} "
                    "(no support for intra-solve interpolation); ignoring.",
                    pybamm.SolverWarning,

$ cd /app/pybamm/packages/pybamm/src/pybamm/solvers && python - <<'EOF'
p = "base_solver.py"
s = open(p).read()
start = s.index("    def _set_sens_initial_conditions_from(\n")
end = s.index("    def _store_first_last_endpoints(self, t_eval):")
new = '''    @staticmethod
    def _mapped_state_sensitivities(
        mapper_func, time, last_state, mapper_inputs: dict, names
    ) -> tuple:
        """Sensitivities of the states that ``mapper_func`` maps a final state 
to.

        By the chain rule, ``dy0/dp = df/dy @ dy/dp + df/dp``, where ``y`` is 
the
        final state of ``last_state`` and ``f`` may depend on ``p`` directly.

        Parameters
        ----------
        mapper_func : :class:`casadi.Function`
            The map ``f(t, y, p)`` to the new initial states, with ``p`` stacked
            as in ``mapper_inputs``.
        time : float
            The time to evaluate the map at.
        last_state : :class:`pybamm.Solution`
            The final state that is mapped.
        mapper_inputs : dict
            The values of ``p``, in the order ``mapper_func`` stacks them.
        names : list of str
            The inputs to differentiate with respect to.

        Returns
        -------
        tuple of :class:`numpy.ndarray`
            The sensitivities of the new initial states, one per name.
        """
        y_from = last_state.all_ys[0]
        dy_from = BaseSolver._state_sensitivities_by_name(last_state, names)
        p = stack_inputs(mapper_inputs)
        _, jac_y, jac_p = mapper_func.jacobian()(
            time, y_from, p, mapper_func(time, y_from, p)
        )

        columns = {}
        start = 0
        for name, value in mapper_inputs.items():
            columns[name] = (start, np.size(value))
            start += np.size(value)

        sensitivities = []
        for name, dy in zip(names, dy_from, strict=True):
            dy0 = jac_y @ casadi.DM(dy)
            if name in columns:
                start, size = columns[name]
                if size != 1:
                    raise pybamm.SolverError(
                        f"Cannot calculate sensitivities to the input '{name}' 
of "
                        f"size {size}, only to scalar inputs"
                    )
                dy0 += jac_p[:, start]
            sensitivities.append(np.asarray(dy0.full()).reshape(-1))
        return tuple(sensitivities)

    def _set_sens_initial_conditions_from(
        self,
        solution: pybamm.Solution,
        model: pybamm.BaseModel,
        inputs: dict | None = None,
    ) -> tuple:
        """
        Sensitivities of the initial states that
        :meth:`pybamm.BaseModel.set_initial_conditions_from` gives a discretised
        model from the final state of a solution.

        Parameters
        ----------
        solution : :class:`pybamm.Solution`
            The solution to use to initialize the model
        model: :class:`pybamm.BaseModel`
            The model whose sensitivities to set
        inputs : dict, optional
            The input parameters of ``model``.

        Returns
        -------
        tuple of :class:`numpy.ndarray`
            The initial conditions for the sensitivities, each element of the 
tuple
            corresponds to an input parameter
        """
        inputs = inputs or {}
        last_state = solution.last_state
        previous_model = last_state.all_models[-1]
        mapper = model.build_initial_state_mapper(previous_model)

        # The map involves the inputs of both models, through their scales and
        # references and any variable of the previous model it reads
        previous_inputs = last_state.all_inputs[0]
        names = sorted(
            {
                symbol.name
                for symbol in mapper.pre_order()
                if isinstance(symbol, pybamm.InputParameter)
            }
        )
        mapper_inputs = {
            name: inputs[name] if name in inputs else previous_inputs[name]
            for name in names
        }

        t_casadi = casadi.MX.sym("t")
        y_casadi = casadi.MX.sym("y", previous_model.len_rhs_and_alg)
        p_casadi = {
            name: casadi.MX.sym(name, np.size(value))
            for name, value in mapper_inputs.items()
        }
        p_casadi_stacked = casadi.vertcat(*p_casadi.values())
        mapper_func = casadi.Function(
            "mapper",
            [t_casadi, y_casadi, p_casadi_stacked],
            [mapper.to_casadi(t_casadi, y_casadi, inputs=p_casadi)],
        )
        return self._mapped_state_sensitivities(
            mapper_func,
            last_state.all_ts[-1][-1],
            last_state,
            mapper_inputs,
            model.calculate_sensitivities,
        )

'''
s = s[:start] + new + s[end:]
open(p, "w").write(s)
EOF

new = '''    @staticmethod
    def _consistent_algebraic_sensitivities(model, time, y0, y0S, inputs_dict):
        """Algebraic sensitivities consistent with the algebraic equations.

        Differentiating ``g(t, y_d, y_a, p) = 0`` gives
        ``dg/dy_a @ dy_a/dp = -(dg/dy_d @ dy_d/dp + dg/dp)``.

        Parameters
        ----------
        model : :class:`pybamm.BaseModel`
            The set-up model.
        time : float
            The time of the states.
        y0 : :class:`numpy.ndarray`
            Consistent states.
        y0S : tuple
            The state sensitivities, one per sensitivity input.
        inputs_dict : dict
            Input parameters.

        Returns
        -------
        tuple of :class:`numpy.ndarray`
            ``y0S`` with consistent algebraic entries.
        """
        len_rhs = model.len_rhs
        inputs = stack_inputs(inputs_dict)
        jac = model.jac_algebraic_eval(time, y0, inputs).sparse()
        jacp = model.jacp_algebraic_eval(time, y0, inputs)
        if isinstance(jacp, casadi.DM):
            jacp = (jacp,)
        jac_differential = jac[:, :len_rhs]
        jac_algebraic = csc_matrix(jac[:, len_rhs:])

        consistent = []
        for sensitivity, dg_dp in zip(y0S, jacp, strict=True):
            if isinstance(sensitivity, casadi.DM):
                sensitivity = sensitivity.full()
            sensitivity = np.array(sensitivity, dtype=float).reshape(-1)
            rhs = jac_differential @ sensitivity[:len_rhs] + 
dg_dp.full().reshape(-1)
            sensitivity[len_rhs:] = -np.atleast_1d(spsolve(jac_algebraic, rhs))
            consistent.append(sensitivity)
        return tuple(consistent)

    def _rhs_dot_consistent_initialization(self, y0, model, time, inputs_dict):
'''

new = '''    @staticmethod
    def _consistent_algebraic_sensitivities(model, time, y0, y0S, inputs_dict):
        """Algebraic sensitivities consistent with the algebraic equations.
        Differentiating ``g(t, y_d, y_a, p) = 0`` gives
        ``dg/dy_a @ dy_a/dp = -(dg/dy_d @ dy_d/dp + dg/dp)``.
        Parameters
        ----------
        model : :class:`pybamm.BaseModel`
            The set-up model.
        time : float
            The time of the states.
        y0 : :class:`numpy.ndarray`
            Consistent states.
        y0S : tuple
            The state sensitivities, one per sensitivity input.
        inputs_dict : dict
            Input parameters.
        Returns
        -------
        tuple of :class:`numpy.ndarray`
            ``y0S`` with consistent algebraic entries.
        """
        len_rhs = model.len_rhs
        inputs = stack_inputs(inputs_dict)
        jac = model.jac_algebraic_eval(time, y0, inputs).sparse()
        jacp = model.jacp_algebraic_eval(time, y0, inputs)
        if isinstance(jacp, casadi.DM):
            jacp = (jacp,)
        jac_differential = jac[:, :len_rhs]
        jac_algebraic = csc_matrix(jac[:, len_rhs:])
        consistent = []
        for sensitivity, dg_dp in zip(y0S, jacp, strict=True):
            if isinstance(sensitivity, casadi.DM):
                sensitivity = sensitivity.full()
            sensitivity = np.array(sensitivity, dtype=float).reshape(-1)
            rhs = jac_differential @ sensitivity[:len_rhs] + 
dg_dp.full().reshape(-1)
            sensitivity[len_rhs:] = -np.atleast_1d(spsolve(jac_algebraic, rhs))
            consistent.append(sensitivity)
        return tuple(consistent)

captured, which is a known limitation and wasn't requested. The sparse Jacobian 
output from the algebraic evaluation is already a scipy CSC matrix, and 
csc_matrix is imported in the solver, so that part checks out. Next I'm setting 
up a DAE sensitivity test where the algebraic sensitivity actually matters, 
using an algebraic variable that jumps at the switch time.

I'm working out the expected sensitivity by hand: the algebraic variable 
contributes 4 before the jump and 6 after it, which gives 34 at t=10. Once the 
switch happens, the sensitivity of a should move from 1 to 5. Next I'll run r2

m = pybamm.BaseModel(); v = pybamm.Variable("v")
m.rhs = {v: pybamm.Modulo(pybamm.t, pybamm.InputParameter("p"))}
m.initial_conditions = {v: 0}; m.variables = {"v": v}
s = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-10)
for p, exp in [(3.0, 29.0), (4.0, 40.0), (np.inf, 200.0)]:
    sol = s.solve(m, [0, 20], inputs={"p": p})
    print(p, sol["v"](20), exp)

    s = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-10, store_first_last=sfl)
    sol = None
    for k in range(4):
        sol = s.step(sol, m, 5.0, inputs={"p": 4.0}, t_interp=np.linspace(0, 5, 
6))

    sol = s.solve(m, [0, 10], inputs={"t_switch": 4.0, "k": 1.0}, 
calculate_sensitivities=["k"])

5")

    for _ in range(2):
        sol = s.step(sol, m, 5.0, inputs={"t_switch": 4.0, "k": 1.0}, 
calculate_sensitivities=["k"])

import pybamm, numpy as np, sys
if len(sys.argv) > 1:
    pybamm.IDAKLUSolver._consistent_algebraic_sensitivities = 
staticmethod(lambda model, time, y0, y0S, inputs: y0S)
m = pybamm.BaseModel()
v = pybamm.Variable("v"); a = pybamm.Variable("a")
ts = pybamm.InputParameter("t_switch"); k = pybamm.InputParameter("k")
m.rhs = {v: a}
m.algebraic = {a: a - k * (1 + 4 * (pybamm.t > ts))}
m.initial_conditions = {v: 0, a: 1}
m.variables = {"v": v, "a": a}
s = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-10, options={"calc_ic": False})
sol = s.solve(m, [0, 10], inputs={"t_switch": 4.0, "k": 1.0}, 
calculate_sensitivities=["k"])
i = np.searchsorted(sol.t, 4.0) 
print(sol.t[i-1:i+2], sol["a"].sensitivities["k"][i-1:i+2].ravel(), 
sol["a"].data[i-1:i+2])
EOF

$ cat > /tmp/repro/r7.py <<'EOF'
import numpy as np, pybamm, sys
pybamm.set_logging_level("ERROR")
name = "Positive particle diffusivity [m2.s-1]"; val = 4e-15
mode = sys.argv[1]
steps = ["Discharge at 1C for 5 minutes", "Hold at 3.7 V for 3 minutes", 
"Discharge at 0.5C for 3 minutes"]
var_pts = {"x_n": 6, "x_s": 6, "x_p": 6, "r_n": 6, "r_p": 6}
def sim(steps=steps):
    model = pybamm.lithium_ion.DFN()
    pv = pybamm.ParameterValues("Chen2020")
    pv.update({name: "[input]"})
    return pybamm.Simulation(model, experiment=pybamm.Experiment(steps), 
parameter_values=pv, experiment_model_mode=mode,
                             var_pts=var_pts, 
solver=pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(rtol=1e-9, atol=1e-11))
def V(p):
    return sim().solve(inputs={name: p})["Voltage [V]"].entries[-1]
h = val * 1e-4
fd = (V(val + h) - V(val - h)) / (2 * h)
sol = sim().solve(inputs={name: val}, calculate_sensitivities=True)
print(mode, "full", sol["Voltage [V]"].sensitivities[name][-1].item(), "fd", fd)
# starting_solution: first step in one sim, rest in another
s1 = sim(steps[:1]).solve(inputs={name: val}, calculate_sensitivities=True)
s2 = sim(steps[1:]).solve(inputs={name: val}, calculate_sensitivities=True, 
starting_solution=s1)
print(mode, "starting_solution", s2["Voltage 
[V]"].sensitivities[name][-1].item(), "fd", fd)
EOF

name = "Positive particle diffusivity [m2.s-1]"; val = 4e-15

steps = ["Discharge at 1C for 5 minutes", "Hold at 3.7 V for 3 minutes", 
"Discharge at 0.5C for 3 minutes"]

def sim(steps=steps):
    model = pybamm.lithium_ion.DFN()
    pv = pybamm.ParameterValues("Chen2020")
    pv.update({name: "[input]"})
    return pybamm.Simulation(model, experiment=pybamm.Experiment(steps), 
parameter_values=pv, experiment_model_mode=mode,
                             var_pts=var_pts, 
solver=pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(rtol=1e-9, atol=1e-11))

s2 = sim(steps[1:]).solve(inputs={name: val}, calculate_sensitivities=True,

$ cat > /tmp/repro/r8.py <<'EOF'
import numpy as np, pybamm
k = pybamm.InputParameter("k"); s = pybamm.InputParameter("s"); r = 
pybamm.InputParameter("r")
def model_a():
    m = pybamm.BaseModel("A")
    u = pybamm.Variable("u")
    m.rhs = {u: -k * u}
    m.initial_conditions = {u: 1.0}
    m.variables = {"u": u, "w": k * u}   # w depends on the input directly
    pybamm.Discretisation().process_model(m)
    return m
def model_b():
    m = pybamm.BaseModel("B")
    u = pybamm.Variable("u", scale=s, reference=r)
    w = pybamm.Variable("w")
    m.rhs = {u: -k * u, w: 0 * w}
    m.initial_conditions = {u: 1.0, w: 0.0}
    m.variables = {"u": u, "w": w}
    pybamm.Discretisation().process_model(m)
    return m
inputs = {"k": 0.7, "s": 3.0, "r": 0.5}
names = ["k", "r", "s"]
for use_mapper in [False, True]:
    try:
        a, b = model_a(), model_b()
        sa = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(rtol=1e-10, atol=1e-12); sb = 
pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(rtol=1e-10, atol=1e-12)
        sol = sa.step(None, a, 1.0, inputs=inputs, 
calculate_sensitivities=names)
        mapper = None
        if use_mapper:
            mapper_expr = b.build_initial_state_mapper(a)
            vfp = pybamm.BaseSolver._get_vars_for_processing(a, {n: inputs[n] 
for n in sorted(ip.name for ip in a.input_parameters)})
            mapper = pybamm.solvers.base_solver.process(mapper_expr, "mapper", 
vfp)[:3]
        sol = sb.step(sol, b, 1.0, inputs=inputs, calculate_sensitivities=names,
state_mapper=mapper)
        t = 2.0
        # u = exp(-k t) physical, du/dk = -t exp(-k t); w = k exp(-k*1) constant
after t=1
        sens = sol.last_state.sensitivities
        print("mapper" if use_mapper else "no mapper")
        print("  u(2)", sol["u"](t), np.exp(-0.7 * t))
        print("  du/dk", sol["u"].sensitivities["k"][-1], -t * np.exp(-0.7 * t))
        print("  du/dr, du/ds", sol["u"].sensitivities["r"][-1], 
sol["u"].sensitivities["s"][-1], "exp 0, 0")
        print("  dw/dk", sol["w"].sensitivities["k"][-1], np.exp(-0.7) * (1 - 
0.7), "w", sol["w"](t), 0.7 * np.exp(-0.7))
    except Exception as e:
        import traceback; traceback.print_exc()
EOF
