import os

# Forbid symbol mutation in this process and its children; must precede pybamm.
os.environ["PYBAMM_TEST_FORBID_SYMBOL_MUTATION"] = "1"

import numpy as np
import pytest
from hypothesis import settings as hypothesis_settings

import pybamm

# Hypothesis auto-loads its built-in "ci" profile in CI, which derandomizes
# every run (the same inputs forever). Re-register it with randomisation
# enabled so CI explores new inputs each run; the inherited print_blob=True
# keeps any failure reproducible locally via @reproduce_failure.
hypothesis_settings.register_profile(
    "ci", parent=hypothesis_settings.get_profile("ci"), derandomize=False
)
if os.environ.get("CI"):
    hypothesis_settings.load_profile("ci")


def pytest_addoption(parser):
    parser.addoption(
        "--scripts",
        action="store_true",
        default=False,
        help="execute the example scripts",
    )
    parser.addoption(
        "--unit", action="store_true", default=False, help="run unit tests"
    )
    parser.addoption(
        "--integration",
        action="store_true",
        default=False,
        help="run integration tests",
    )


def pytest_configure(config):
    config.addinivalue_line("markers", "scripts: mark test as an example 
script")
    config.addinivalue_line("markers", "unit: mark test as a unit test")
    config.addinivalue_line("markers", "integration: mark test as an integration
test")
    config.addinivalue_line("markers", "memory: mark test as a memory stress 
test")
    config.addinivalue_line("markers", "time_bench: mark test as a timing 
benchmark")
    config.addinivalue_line("markers", "memory_bench: mark test as a memory 
benchmark")
    config.addinivalue_line(
        "markers", "slow_bench: mark benchmark as run on a schedule, not per 
push"
    )


def pytest_collection_modifyitems(items):
    for item in items:
        if "benchmarks" in item.nodeid:
            pass  # benchmarks carry time_bench/memory_bench markers explicitly
        elif "unit" in item.nodeid:
            item.add_marker(pytest.mark.unit)
        elif "integration" in item.nodeid:
            item.add_marker(pytest.mark.integration)
        elif "memory" in item.nodeid:
            item.add_marker(pytest.mark.memory)

cat > /tmp/teststubs/pytest_mock_stub.py <<'EOF'
import pytest
from unittest import mock

class MockerFixture:
    Mock = mock.Mock; MagicMock = mock.MagicMock; ANY = mock.ANY; call = 
mock.call
    def __init__(self): self._patches = []
    def _start(self, p):
        self._patches.append(p); return p.start()
    def patch(self, *a, **k): return self._start(mock.patch(*a, **k))
    def spy(self, obj, name):
        orig = getattr(obj, name)
        return self._start(mock.patch.object(obj, name, side_effect=orig, 
autospec=True)) 
    def stopall(self):
        for p in reversed(self._patches): p.stop()
def _object(self, *a, **k): return self._start(mock.patch.object(*a, **k))
def _dict(self, *a, **k): return self._start(mock.patch.dict(*a, **k))
class _P:
    def __init__(self, m): self.m = m
    def __call__(self, *a, **k): return self.m._start(mock.patch(*a, **k))
    def object(self, *a, **k): return self.m._start(mock.patch.object(*a, **k))
    def dict(self, *a, **k): return self.m._start(mock.patch.dict(*a, **k))
@pytest.fixture
def mocker():
    m = MockerFixture(); m.patch = _P(m)
    yield m
    m.stopall()
EOF

$ cat > /tmp/repro/r9.py <<'EOF'
import numpy as np, pybamm
def model(kind):
    m = pybamm.BaseModel(); v = pybamm.Variable("v")
    if kind == "const":
        m.rhs = {v: 1 + (pybamm.t > 5.0)}
    elif kind == "input":
        m.rhs = {v: 1 + (pybamm.t > pybamm.InputParameter("ts"))}
    elif kind == "ge":
        m.rhs = {v: 1 + (pybamm.t >= 5.0)}
    elif kind == "mod":
        m.rhs = {v: pybamm.Modulo(pybamm.t, 5.0)}
    elif kind == "modin":
        m.rhs = {v: pybamm.Modulo(pybamm.t, pybamm.InputParameter("ts"))}
    m.initial_conditions = {v: 0}; m.variables = {"v": v}
    return m
exp = {"const": 15.0, "input": 15.0, "ge": 15.0, "mod": 12.5 + 12.5, "modin": 
25.0}
prev = lambda x: np.nextafter(x, -np.inf)
nxt = lambda x: np.nextafter(x, np.inf)
for kind in exp:
    for first_dt in [5.0, prev(5.0), prev(prev(5.0)), prev(prev(prev(5.0))), 
nxt(5.0), 5.0 * (1 - 2.2e-16)]:
        for opts in [dict(), dict(t_interp=True), dict(sfl=True)]:
            try:
                s = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(rtol=1e-10, atol=1e-12, 
store_first_last=opts.get("sfl", False))
                m = model(kind)
                sol = s.step(None, m, first_dt, inputs={"ts": 5.0})
                dt2 = 10.0 - sol.t[-1]
                kw = {}
                if opts.get("t_interp"):
                    kw["t_interp"] = np.linspace(0, dt2, 7)
                sol = s.step(sol, m, dt2, inputs={"ts": 5.0}, **kw)
                val = sol["v"](sol.t[-1])
                ok = abs(val - exp[kind]) < 1e-6
                print(kind, repr(first_dt), opts, "OK" if ok else f"BAD {val} vs
{exp[kind]}")
            except Exception as e:
                print(kind, repr(first_dt), opts, "EXC", type(e).__name__, 
str(e)[:150])
EOF

import numpy as np, pybamm
def model(kind):
    m = pybamm.BaseModel(); v = pybamm.Variable("v")
    if kind == "const":
        m.rhs = {v: 1 + (pybamm.t > 5.0)}
    elif kind == "input":
        m.rhs = {v: 1 + (pybamm.t > pybamm.InputParameter("ts"))}
    elif kind == "ge":
        m.rhs = {v: 1 + (pybamm.t >= 5.0)}
    elif kind == "mod":
        m.rhs = {v: pybamm.Modulo(pybamm.t, 5.0)}
    elif kind == "modin":
        m.rhs = {v: pybamm.Modulo(pybamm.t, pybamm.InputParameter("ts"))}
    m.initial_conditions = {v: 0}; m.variables = {"v": v}
    return m
exp = {"const": 15.0, "input": 15.0, "ge": 15.0, "mod": 12.5 + 12.5, "modin": 
25.0}
prev = lambda x: np.nextafter(x, -np.inf)
nxt = lambda x: np.nextafter(x, np.inf)
for kind in exp:
    for first_dt in [5.0, prev(5.0), prev(prev(5.0)), prev(prev(prev(5.0))), 
nxt(5.0), 5.0 * (1 - 2.2e-16)]:
        for opts in [dict(), dict(t_interp=True), dict(sfl=True)]:
            try:
                s = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(rtol=1e-10, atol=1e-12, 
store_first_last=opts.get("sfl", False))
                m = model(kind)
                sol = s.step(None, m, first_dt, inputs={"ts": 5.0})
                dt2 = 10.0 - sol.t[-1]
                kw = {}
                if opts.get("t_interp"):
                    kw["t_interp"] = np.linspace(0, dt2, 7)
                sol = s.step(sol, m, dt2, inputs={"ts": 5.0}, **kw)
                val = sol["v"](sol.t[-1])
                ok = abs(val - exp[kind]) < 1e-6
                print(kind, repr(first_dt), opts, "OK" if ok else f"BAD {val} vs
{exp[kind]}")
            except Exception as e:
                print(kind, repr(first_dt), opts, "EXC", type(e).__name__, 
str(e)[:150])

+    @staticmethod
+    def _consistent_algebraic_sensitivities(model, time, y0, y0S, inputs_dict):
+        """Algebraic sensitivities consistent with the algebraic equations.
+
+        Differentiating ``g(t, y_d, y_a, p) = 0`` gives
+        ``dg/dy_a @ dy_a/dp = -(dg/dy_d @ dy_d/dp + dg/dp)``.
+
+        Parameters
+        ----------
+        model : :class:`pybamm.BaseModel`
+            The set-up model.
+        time : float
+            The time of the states.
+        y0 : :class:`numpy.ndarray`
+            Consistent states.
+        y0S : tuple
+            The state sensitivities, one per sensitivity input.
+        inputs_dict : dict
+            Input parameters.
+
+        Returns
+        -------
+        tuple of :class:`numpy.ndarray`
+            ``y0S`` with consistent algebraic entries.
+        """
+        len_rhs = model.len_rhs
+        inputs = stack_inputs(inputs_dict)
+        jac = model.jac_algebraic_eval(time, y0, inputs).sparse()
+        jacp = model.jacp_algebraic_eval(time, y0, inputs)
+        if isinstance(jacp, casadi.DM):
+            jacp = (jacp,)
+        jac_differential = jac[:, :len_rhs]
+        jac_algebraic = csc_matrix(jac[:, len_rhs:])
+
+        consistent = []
+        for sensitivity, dg_dp in zip(y0S, jacp, strict=True):
+            if isinstance(sensitivity, casadi.DM):
+                sensitivity = sensitivity.full()
+            sensitivity = np.array(sensitivity, dtype=float).reshape(-1)
+            rhs = jac_differential @ sensitivity[:len_rhs] + 
dg_dp.full().reshape(-1)
+            sensitivity[len_rhs:] = -np.atleast_1d(spsolve(jac_algebraic, rhs))
+            consistent.append(sensitivity)
+        return tuple(consistent)
+

+    def _integrate_segments(
+        self,
+        model,
+        t_eval,
+        start_indices,
+        end_indices,
+        inputs_list,
+        t_interp,
+        nproc,
+        timer,
+    ):
+        """Integrate each input set across the segments between 
discontinuities.
+
+        Each input set restarts every segment from the state and sensitivities
+        it reached at the end of the previous one, and stops at the first
+        segment it does not integrate to the end of.
+
+        Parameters
+        ----------
+        model : :class:`pybamm.BaseModel`
+            The set-up model, initialised for every input set.
+        t_eval : :class:`numpy.ndarray`
+            The integration times of all the segments.
+        start_indices, end_indices : list of int
+            The bounds of each segment in ``t_eval``.
+        inputs_list : list of dict
+            Input parameters for each input set.
+        t_interp : :class:`numpy.ndarray`
+            The interpolation times, possibly empty.
+        nproc : int or None
+            Number of processes to integrate the input sets with.
+        timer : :class:`pybamm.Timer`
+            The timer that the segments' solve times are read from.
+
+        Returns
+        -------
+        list of :class:`pybamm.Solution`
+            One solution per input set.
+        """
+        solutions = [None] * len(inputs_list)
+        active = list(range(len(inputs_list)))
+        new_solutions = []
+        for start_index, end_index in zip(start_indices, end_indices, 
strict=True):
+            t_segment = t_eval[start_index:end_index]
+            active_inputs = [inputs_list[i] for i in active]
+            if start_index != start_indices[0]:
+                self._restart_from_solutions(
+                    model, t_segment[0], new_solutions, active_inputs
+                )
+            pybamm.logger.verbose(
+                f"Calling solver for {t_segment[0]} < t < {t_segment[-1]}"
+            )
+            new_solutions = self._integrate(
+                model,
+                t_segment,
+                active_inputs,
+                self._segment_t_interp(t_interp, t_segment),
+                nproc=nproc,
+            )
+            # pybamm.Solution.__add__ assumes attribute solve_time.
+            solve_time = timer.time()
+            for i, new_solution in zip(active, new_solutions, strict=True):
+                new_solution.solve_time = solve_time
+                if solutions[i] is None:
+                    solutions[i] = new_solution
+                else:
+                    solutions[i] = solutions[i] + new_solution
+
+            still_active = [
+                k
+                for k, i in enumerate(active)
+                if solutions[i].termination == "final time"
+            ]
+            active = [active[k] for k in still_active]
+            new_solutions = [new_solutions[k] for k in still_active]
+            if not active:
+                break
+        return solutions
+
+    @staticmethod
+    def _segment_t_interp(t_interp, t_segment):
+        """The interpolation times within one segment.
+
+        A segment without any keeps its own end points, since an empty
+        ``t_interp`` asks the solver for every step instead.
+        """
+        if len(t_interp) == 0:
+            return t_interp
+        t_interp = np.asarray(t_interp, dtype=float)
+        in_segment = t_interp[(t_interp >= t_segment[0]) & (t_interp <= 
t_segment[-1])]
+        if in_segment.size == 0:
+            return np.array([t_segment[0], t_segment[-1]])
+        return in_segment
+
+    def _restart_from_solutions(self, model, time, solutions, inputs_list):
+        """Initialise ``model`` from the final states of ``solutions``.
+
+        Parameters
+        ----------
+        model : :class:`pybamm.BaseModel`
+            The set-up model.
+        time : float
+            The time to restart at.
+        solutions : list of :class:`pybamm.Solution`
+            The solution each input set restarts from.
+        inputs_list : list of dict
+            Input parameters for each input set.
+        """
+        names = model.calculate_sensitivities
+        y0_list = []
+        y0S_list = []
+        for solution in solutions:
+            last_state = solution.last_state
+            y0 = last_state.all_ys[0]
+            if isinstance(y0, casadi.DM):
+                y0 = y0.full()
+            y0_list.append(np.asarray(y0)[:, -1])
+            if names and model.y0S_list is not None:
+                y0S_list.append(self._state_sensitivities_by_name(last_state, 
names))
+        model.y0_list = y0_list
+        if y0S_list:
+            model.y0S_list = y0S_list
+        self._set_consistent_initialization(model, time, inputs_list, 
restart=True)
+
+    @staticmethod
+    def _state_sensitivities_by_name(last_state, names) -> tuple:
+        """The final state sensitivities of a solution to each input in 
``names``.
+
+        Parameters
+        ----------
+        last_state : :class:`pybamm.Solution`
+            The final state of a solution.
+        names : list of str
+            The inputs to differentiate with respect to.
+
+        Returns
+        -------
+        tuple of :class:`numpy.ndarray`
+            One flat state sensitivity per name.
+
+        Raises
+        ------
+        :class:`pybamm.SolverError`
+            If the solution has no sensitivities to some of ``names``.
+        """
+        sensitivities = last_state._all_sensitivities
+        missing = [name for name in names if name not in sensitivities]
+        if missing:
+            raise pybamm.SolverError(
+                "Cannot continue a sensitivity solve from a solution that has 
no "
+                f"sensitivities to the inputs {missing}. Solve it with these 
inputs "
+                "in 'calculate_sensitivities', or start a new solve."
+            )
+        n_states = last_state.all_models[-1].len_rhs_and_alg
+        y0S = []
+        for name in names:
+            value = sensitivities[name]
+            if isinstance(value, list):
+                value = value[-1]
+            y0S.append(np.asarray(value, dtype=float).reshape(-1)[-n_states:])
+        return tuple(y0S)
+

         """

$ cat > /tmp/repro/r10.py <<'EOF'
import numpy as np, pybamm, warnings
warnings.simplefilter("ignore")
pybamm.set_logging_level("ERROR")
def run(name, f):
    try: print(name, "->", f())
    except Exception as e:
        import traceback; traceback.print_exc(); print(name, "-> EXC", 
type(e).__name__, str(e)[:300])
def f_resolve(mode):
    pv = pybamm.ParameterValues("Chen2020"); pv.update({"Positive particle 
diffusivity [m2.s-1]": "[input]"})
    sim = pybamm.Simulation(pybamm.lithium_ion.SPM(), parameter_values=pv, 
experiment_model_mode=mode,
        experiment=pybamm.Experiment(["Discharge at 1C for 5 minutes", "Rest for
2 minutes", "Hold at 3.9 V for 1 minute"]))
    inp = {"Positive particle diffusivity [m2.s-1]": 4e-15}
    a = sim.solve(inputs=inp, calculate_sensitivities=True)
    b = sim.solve(inputs=inp)
    c = sim.solve(inputs=inp, calculate_sensitivities=True)
    return a["Voltage [V]"].entries[-1], b["Voltage [V]"].entries[-1], 
c["Voltage [V]"].sensitivities["Positive particle diffusivity [m2.s-1]"][-1]
run("resolve legacy", lambda: f_resolve("legacy"))
run("resolve unified", lambda: f_resolve("unified"))
def ode():
    m = pybamm.BaseModel(); v = pybamm.Variable("v"); u = pybamm.Variable("u")
    ts = pybamm.InputParameter("ts"); k = pybamm.InputParameter("k")
    m.rhs = {v: k * (1 + (pybamm.t > ts)), u: pybamm.Modulo(pybamm.t, 3.0)}
    m.initial_conditions = {v: 0, u: 0}; m.variables = {"v": v, "u": u, "2v": 2 
* v}
    return m
def f_outvars():
    s = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(output_variables=["2v"], rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-10)
    sol = s.solve(ode(), [0, 10], inputs={"ts": 4.0, "k": 2.0}, 
calculate_sensitivities=["k"])
    return sol["2v"](10), "exp", 2 * 2 * 16, sol["2v"].sensitivities["k"][-1], 
"exp", 32
run("outvars", f_outvars)
def f_casadi():
    s = pybamm.CasadiSolver(rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-10)
    sols = s.solve(ode(), np.linspace(0, 10, 11), inputs=[{"ts": 4.0, "k": 2.0},
{"ts": 4.0, "k": 1.0}])
    st = None
    for _ in range(2):
        st = s.step(st, ode(), 5.0, inputs={"ts": 4.0, "k": 2.0}) if False else 
st
    return [x["v"](10) for x in sols], "exp 32, 16", [x["u"](10) for x in sols],
"exp", 13.5 + 0.5
run("casadi", f_casadi)
def f_nproc():
    s = pybamm.CasadiSolver(rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-10)
    sols = s.solve(ode(), np.linspace(0, 10, 11), inputs=[{"ts": 4.0, "k": 2.0},
{"ts": 4.0, "k": 1.0}], nproc=2)
    return [x["v"](10) for x in sols]
run("nproc", f_nproc)
def f_multi_idaklu():
    s = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-10, options={"num_threads": 2})
    sols = s.solve(ode(), np.linspace(0, 10, 11), inputs=[{"ts": 4.0, "k": 2.0},
{"ts": 4.0, "k": 1.0}], calculate_sensitivities=True)
    return [x["v"](10) for x in sols], [x["v"].sensitivities["k"][-1] for x in 
sols], "exp 16 16"
run("multi idaklu", f_multi_idaklu)
def f_events_sim():
    m = ode()
    s = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver()
    for sens in [False, True, False, ["k"]]:
        s.solve(m, [0, 10], inputs={"ts": 4.0, "k": 2.0}, 
calculate_sensitivities=sens)
    s2 = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver()
    s2.step(None, m, 5, inputs={"ts": 4.0, "k": 2.0})
    return len(m.events), [e.name for e in m.events]
run("events", f_events_sim)
def f_scipy():
    s = pybamm.ScipySolver(rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-10)
    sol = s.solve(ode(), np.linspace(0, 10, 11), inputs={"ts": 4.0, "k": 2.0})
    return sol["v"](10), sol["u"](10)
run("scipy", f_scipy)
EOF

        s.solve(m, [0, 10], inputs={"ts": 4.0, "k": 2.0}, 
calculate_sensitivities=sens)
    s2 = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver()
    s2.step(None, m, 5, inputs={"ts": 4.0, "k": 2.0})
    return len(m.events), [e.name for e in m.events]
run("events", f_events_sim)
def f_scipy():
    s = pybamm.ScipySolver(rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-10)
    sol = s.solve(ode(), np.linspace(0, 10, 11), inputs={"ts": 4.0, "k": 2.0})
    return sol["v"](10), sol["u"](10)
run("scipy", f_scipy)
EOF

def _input_switch_model(algebraic=False):
    # v' = k (1 + 4 H(t - t_switch)), directly or through an algebraic state a
    model = pybamm.BaseModel()
    v = pybamm.Variable("v")
    k = pybamm.InputParameter("k")
    rate = k * (1 + 4 * (pybamm.t > pybamm.InputParameter("t_switch")))
    if algebraic:
        a = pybamm.Variable("a")
        model.rhs = {v: a}
        model.algebraic = {a: a - rate}
        model.initial_conditions = {v: 0, a: 1}
        model.variables = {"v": v, "a": a}
    else:
        model.rhs = {v: rate}
        model.initial_conditions = {v: 0}
        model.variables = {"v": v}
    return model


def _modulo_model(period):
    model = pybamm.BaseModel()
    v = pybamm.Variable("v")
    model.rhs = {v: pybamm.Modulo(pybamm.t, period)}
    model.initial_conditions = {v: 0}
    model.variables = {"v": v}
    return model


def _integral_of_modulo(t, period):
    # integral of s mod period over [0, t]
    if np.isinf(period):
        return t**2 / 2
    return (t // period) * period**2 / 2 + (t % period) ** 2 / 2


class TestIDAKLUDiscontinuityRestarts:
    @pytest.mark.parametrize("algebraic", [False, True])
    @pytest.mark.parametrize("calc_ic", [True, False])
    def test_solve_continues_from_state_at_input_timed_discontinuity(
        self, algebraic, calc_ic
    ):
        solver = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(
            rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-10, options={"calc_ic": calc_ic}
        )
        solution = solver.solve(
            _input_switch_model(algebraic),
            [0, 10],
            inputs={"t_switch": 4.0, "k": 2.0},
            calculate_sensitivities=["k"],
        )

        # v(10) = k (4 + 5 * 6), so dv/dk = 34
        np.testing.assert_allclose(solution["v"](10), 68, rtol=1e-8)
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            solution["v"].sensitivities["k"][-1], 34, rtol=1e-8
        )
        if algebraic:
            # The algebraic sensitivity is consistent right after the jump
            after = np.searchsorted(solution.t, 4.0, side="right")
            np.testing.assert_allclose(
                solution["a"].sensitivities["k"][after], 5, rtol=1e-8
            )

    @pytest.mark.parametrize(
        "t_eval", [np.array([0, 10]), np.arange(11), [0, 10]], ids=str
    )
    def test_solve_integer_t_eval_is_not_modified(self, t_eval):
        t_eval_before = np.array(t_eval).copy()
        solution = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver().solve(
            _input_switch_model(), t_eval, inputs={"t_switch": 4.0, "k": 1.0}
        )
        np.testing.assert_allclose(solution["v"](10), 34, rtol=1e-6)
        np.testing.assert_array_equal(t_eval, t_eval_before)

    def test_solve_input_sets_sharing_discontinuity_on_t_eval_point(self):
        t_eval = np.linspace(0, 10, 11)
        solutions = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver().solve(
            _input_switch_model(),
            t_eval,
            inputs=[{"t_switch": 4.0, "k": 1.0}, {"t_switch": 4.0, "k": 2.0}],
        )
        for solution, k in zip(solutions, [1.0, 2.0], strict=True):
            np.testing.assert_allclose(solution["v"](10), 34 * k, rtol=1e-6)
        np.testing.assert_array_equal(t_eval, np.linspace(0, 10, 11))

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("store_first_last", [False, True])
    def test_solve_with_t_interp_across_discontinuity(self, store_first_last):
        solver = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(store_first_last=store_first_last)
        t_interp = None if store_first_last else np.linspace(0, 10, 21)
        solution = solver.solve(
            _input_switch_model(),
            [0, 10],
            inputs={"t_switch": 4.0, "k": 1.0},
            t_interp=t_interp,
        )
        np.testing.assert_allclose(solution["v"](10), 34, rtol=1e-6)
        assert solution.t[0] == 0
        assert solution.t[-1] == 10

    def test_solve_input_set_terminating_before_discontinuity(self):
        model = _input_switch_model()
        v = model.variables["v"]
        model.events = [pybamm.Event("v limit", pybamm.InputParameter("v_max") -
v)]
        solutions = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver().solve(
            model,
            [0, 10],
            inputs=[
                {"t_switch": 4.0, "k": 1.0, "v_max": 2.0},
                {"t_switch": 4.0, "k": 1.0, "v_max": 100.0},
            ],
        )
        assert solutions[0].termination == "event: v limit"
        np.testing.assert_allclose(solutions[0].t[-1], 2.0, rtol=1e-6)
        assert solutions[1].termination == "final time"
        assert solutions[1].t[-1] == 10
        np.testing.assert_allclose(solutions[1]["v"](10), 34, rtol=1e-6)

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("calc_ic", [True, False])
    def test_step_across_algebraic_jump(self, calc_ic):
        model = _input_switch_model(algebraic=True)
        solver = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(
            rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-10, options={"calc_ic": calc_ic}
        )
        solution = solver.step(
            None,
            model,
            10,
            inputs={"t_switch": 4.0, "k": 1.0},
            calculate_sensitivities=["k"],
        )
        np.testing.assert_allclose(solution["v"](10), 34, rtol=1e-8)
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            solution["v"].sensitivities["k"][-1], 34, rtol=1e-8
        )
        assert np.any(np.isclose(solution.t, 4.0, rtol=1e-12, atol=0))

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("period", [3.0, pybamm.InputParameter("period")])
    def test_step_stops_at_modulo_resets(self, period):
        solver = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-10)
        model = _modulo_model(period)
        solution = None
        for _ in range(2):
            solution = solver.step(solution, model, 10, inputs={"period": 3.0})
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            solution["v"](20), _integral_of_modulo(20, 3.0), rtol=1e-7
        )
        for reset in [3, 6, 9, 12, 15, 18]:
            assert np.any(np.isclose(solution.t, reset, rtol=1e-12, atol=0))

    def test_step_stops_at_constant_discontinuity(self):
        model = pybamm.BaseModel()
        v = pybamm.Variable("v")
        model.rhs = {v: 1 + (pybamm.t > 5.0)}
        model.initial_conditions = {v: 0}
        model.variables = {"v": v}
        solution = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver().step(None, model, 10)
        assert 5.0 in solution.t
        np.testing.assert_allclose(solution["v"](10), 15, rtol=1e-6)

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("store_first_last", [False, True])
    @pytest.mark.parametrize("ulps_before", [0, 1, 2, 3])
    def test_step_starting_within_a_float_of_a_discontinuity(
        self, store_first_last, ulps_before
    ):
        solver = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(
            rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-10, store_first_last=store_first_last
        )
        model = _input_switch_model()
        inputs = {"t_switch": 4.0, "k": 1.0}
        first_dt = 4.0
        for _ in range(ulps_before):
            first_dt = np.nextafter(first_dt, -np.inf)
        solution = solver.step(None, model, first_dt, inputs=inputs)
        dt = 10 - solution.t[-1]
        solution = solver.step(
            solution, model, dt, inputs=inputs, t_interp=np.linspace(0, dt, 5)
        )
        np.testing.assert_allclose(solution["v"](solution.t[-1]), 34, rtol=1e-7)

    @pytest.mark.parametrize(
        ("period", "expected"),
        [(3.0, _integral_of_modulo(20, 3.0)), (4.0, 40.0), (np.inf, 200.0)],
    )
    def test_modulo_with_input_period(self, period, expected):
        solver = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-10)
        model = _modulo_model(pybamm.InputParameter("period"))
        # The same solver resets wherever each input set's period puts it
        for value, result in [(period, expected), (5.0, 50.0)]:
            solution = solver.solve(model, [0, 20], inputs={"period": value})
            np.testing.assert_allclose(solution["v"](20), result, rtol=1e-6)

    def test_modulo_with_time_dependent_period(self):
        with pytest.raises(pybamm.SolverError, match="time-dependent period"):
            pybamm.IDAKLUSolver().solve(_modulo_model(1 + pybamm.t), [0, 1])

    def test_step_does_not_reuse_other_input_sensitivities(self):
        model = pybamm.BaseModel()
        u = pybamm.Variable("u")
        a = pybamm.InputParameter("a")
        b = pybamm.InputParameter("b")
        model.rhs = {u: a + 2 * b}
        model.initial_conditions = {u: 0}
        model.variables = {"u": u}
        inputs = {"a": 1.0, "b": 1.0}
        solution = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver().step(
            None, model, 1, inputs=inputs, calculate_sensitivities=["a"]
        )
        with pytest.raises(pybamm.SolverError, match=r"\['b'\]"):
            pybamm.IDAKLUSolver().step(
                solution, model, 1, inputs=inputs, calculate_sensitivities=["b"]
            )

        solution = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver().step(
            None, model, 1, inputs=inputs, calculate_sensitivities=["b", "a"]
        )
        solution = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver().step(
            solution, model, 1, inputs=inputs, calculate_sensitivities=["b"]
        )
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            solution["u"].sensitivities["b"][-1], 4, rtol=1e-10
        )

    def test_step_changing_calculate_sensitivities(self):
        model = pybamm.BaseModel()
        u = pybamm.Variable("u")
        model.rhs = {u: pybamm.InputParameter("a")}
        model.initial_conditions = {u: 0}
        model.variables = {"u": u}
        solver = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver()
        inputs = {"a": 2.0}

        solution = solver.step(None, model, 1, inputs=inputs, 
calculate_sensitivities=True)
        solution = solver.step(solution, model, 1, inputs=inputs)
        np.testing.assert_allclose(solution["u"](2), 4, rtol=1e-10)

        solution = solver.step(None, model, 1, inputs=inputs, 
calculate_sensitivities=True)
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            solution["u"].sensitivities["a"][-1], 1, rtol=1e-10
        )

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("use_mapper", [False, True])
    def test_step_sensitivities_across_change_of_model(self, use_mapper):
        k, scale, reference = (pybamm.InputParameter(n) for n in "ksr")

        def model_with(new_model):
            model = pybamm.BaseModel()
            u = pybamm.Variable(
                "u",
                scale=scale if new_model else 1,
                reference=reference if new_model else 0,
            )
            z = pybamm.Variable("z")
            model.rhs = {u: -k * u, z: scale + reference}
            model.initial_conditions = {u: 1.0, z: 0.0}
            model.variables = {"u": u, "z": z}
            if new_model:
                w = pybamm.Variable("w")
                model.rhs[w] = pybamm.Scalar(0)
                model.initial_conditions[w] = 0.0
                model.variables["w"] = w
            else:
                # w starts the new model from a value depending on k directly
                model.variables["w"] = k * u
            pybamm.Discretisation().process_model(model)
            return model

        previous_model, model = model_with(False), model_with(True)
        inputs = {"k": 0.7, "s": 3.0, "r": 0.5}
        names = ["k", "r", "s"]
        solution = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(rtol=1e-10, atol=1e-12).step(
            None, previous_model, 1.0, inputs=inputs, 
calculate_sensitivities=names
        )
        state_mapper = None
        if use_mapper:
            mapper = model.build_initial_state_mapper(previous_model)
            vars_for_processing = pybamm.BaseSolver._get_vars_for_processing(
                previous_model, inputs
            )
            state_mapper = pybamm.solvers.base_solver.process(
                mapper, "mapper", vars_for_processing
            )[:3]
        solution = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(rtol=1e-10, atol=1e-12).step(
            solution,
            model,
            1.0,
            inputs=inputs,
            calculate_sensitivities=names,
            state_mapper=state_mapper,
        )

        t = 2.0
        u_sensitivities = solution["u"].sensitivities
        np.testing.assert_allclose(solution["u"](t), np.exp(-0.7 * t), 
rtol=1e-8)
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            u_sensitivities["k"][-1], -t * np.exp(-0.7 * t), rtol=1e-7
        )
        np.testing.assert_allclose(u_sensitivities["r"][-1], 0, atol=1e-10)
        np.testing.assert_allclose(u_sensitivities["s"][-1], 0, atol=1e-10)
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            solution["w"].sensitivities["k"][-1], 0.3 * np.exp(-0.7), rtol=1e-7
        )

def _input_switch_model(algebraic=False):
    # v' = k (1 + 4 H(t - t_switch)), directly or through an algebraic state a
    model = pybamm.BaseModel()
    v = pybamm.Variable("v")
    k = pybamm.InputParameter("k")
    rate = k * (1 + 4 * (pybamm.t > pybamm.InputParameter("t_switch")))
    if algebraic:
        a = pybamm.Variable("a")
        model.rhs = {v: a}
        model.algebraic = {a: a - rate}
        model.initial_conditions = {v: 0, a: 1}
        model.variables = {"v": v, "a": a}
    else:
        model.rhs = {v: rate}
        model.initial_conditions = {v: 0}
        model.variables = {"v": v}
    return model
.def _modulo_model(period):
    model = pybamm.BaseModel()
    v = pybamm.Variable("v")
    model.rhs = {v: pybamm.Modulo(pybamm.t, period)}
    model.initial_conditions = {v: 0}
    model.variables = {"v": v}
    return model
 def _integral_of_modulo(t, period):
    # integral of s mod period over [0, t]
    if np.isinf(period):
        return t**2 / 2
    return (t // period) * period**2 / 2 + (t % period) ** 2 / 2
class TestIDAKLUDiscontinuityRestarts:
    @pytest.mark.parametrize("algebraic", [False, True])
    @pytest.mark.parametrize("calc_ic", [True, False])
    def test_solve_continues_from_state_at_input_timed_discontinuity(
        self, algebraic, calc_ic
    ):
        solver = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(
            rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-10, options={"calc_ic": calc_ic}
        )
        solution = solver.solve(
            _input_switch_model(algebraic),
            [0, 10],
            inputs={"t_switch": 4.0, "k": 2.0},
            calculate_sensitivities=["k"],
        )
        # v(10) = k (4 + 5 * 6), so dv/dk = 34
        np.testing.assert_allclose(solution["v"](10), 68, rtol=1e-8)
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            solution["v"].sensitivities["k"][-1], 34, rtol=1e-8
        )
        if algebraic:
            # The algebraic sensitivity is consistent right after the jump
            after = np.searchsorted(solution.t, 4.0, side="right")
            np.testing.assert_allclose(
                solution["a"].sensitivities["k"][after], 5, rtol=1e-8
            )
    @pytest.mark.parametrize(
        "t_eval", [np.array([0, 10]), np.arange(11), [0, 10]], ids=str
    )
    def test_solve_integer_t_eval_is_not_modified(self, t_eval):
        t_eval_before = np.array(t_eval).copy()
        solution = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver().solve(
            _input_switch_model(), t_eval, inputs={"t_switch": 4.0, "k": 1.0}
        )
        np.testing.assert_allclose(solution["v"](10), 34, rtol=1e-6)
        np.testing.assert_array_equal(t_eval, t_eval_before)
    def test_solve_input_sets_sharing_discontinuity_on_t_eval_point(self):
        t_eval = np.linspace(0, 10, 11)
        solutions = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver().solve(
            _input_switch_model(),
            t_eval,
            inputs=[{"t_switch": 4.0, "k": 1.0}, {"t_switch": 4.0, "k": 2.0}],
        )
        for solution, k in zip(solutions, [1.0, 2.0], strict=True):
            np.testing.assert_allclose(solution["v"](10), 34 * k, rtol=1e-6)
        np.testing.assert_array_equal(t_eval, np.linspace(0, 10, 11))
.    @pytest.mark.parametrize("store_first_last", [False, True])
    def test_solve_with_t_interp_across_discontinuity(self, store_first_last):
        solver = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(store_first_last=store_first_last)
        t_interp = None if store_first_last else np.linspace(0, 10, 21)
        solution = solver.solve(
            _input_switch_model(),
            [0, 10],
            inputs={"t_switch": 4.0, "k": 1.0},
            t_interp=t_interp,
        )
        np.testing.assert_allclose(solution["v"](10), 34, rtol=1e-6)
        assert solution.t[0] == 0
        assert solution.t[-1] == 10
    def test_solve_input_set_terminating_before_discontinuity(self):
        model = _input_switch_model()
        v = model.variables["v"]
        model.events = [pybamm.Event("v limit", pybamm.InputParameter("v_max") -
v)]
        solutions = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver().solve(
            model,
            [0, 10],
            inputs=[
                {"t_switch": 4.0, "k": 1.0, "v_max": 2.0},
                {"t_switch": 4.0, "k": 1.0, "v_max": 100.0},
            ],
        )
        assert solutions[0].termination == "event: v limit"
        np.testing.assert_allclose(solutions[0].t[-1], 2.0, rtol=1e-6)
        assert solutions[1].termination == "final time"
        assert solutions[1].t[-1] == 10
        np.testing.assert_allclose(solutions[1]["v"](10), 34, rtol=1e-6)

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("period", [3.0, pybamm.InputParameter("period")])
    def test_step_stops_at_modulo_resets(self, period):
        solver = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-10)
        model = _modulo_model(period)
        solution = None
        for _ in range(2):
            solution = solver.step(solution, model, 10, inputs={"period": 3.0})
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            solution["v"](20), _integral_of_modulo(20, 3.0), rtol=1e-7
        )
        for reset in [3, 6, 9, 12, 15, 18]:
            assert np.any(np.isclose(solution.t, reset, rtol=1e-12, atol=0))
(    def test_step_stops_at_constant_discontinuity(self):
        model = pybamm.BaseModel()

n    @pytest.mark.parametrize("store_first_last", [False, True])
    @pytest.mark.parametrize("ulps_before", [0, 1, 2, 3])
    def test_step_starting_within_a_float_of_a_discontinuity(
        self, store_first_last, ulps_before
    ):
        solver = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(
            rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-10, store_first_last=store_first_last
        )
        model = _input_switch_model()
        inputs = {"t_switch": 4.0, "k": 1.0}
        first_dt = 4.0
        for _ in range(ulps_before):
            first_dt = np.nextafter(first_dt, -np.inf)
        solution = solver.step(None, model, first_dt, inputs=inputs)
        dt = 10 - solution.t[-1]
        solution = solver.step(
            solution, model, dt, inputs=inputs, t_interp=np.linspace(0, dt, 5)
        )
        np.testing.assert_allclose(solution["v"](solution.t[-1]), 34, rtol=1e-7)
u    @pytest.mark.parametrize(
        ("period", "expected"),
        [(3.0, _integral_of_modulo(20, 3.0)), (4.0, 40.0), (np.inf, 200.0)],
    )
    def test_modulo_with_input_period(self, period, expected):
        solver = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-10)
        model = _modulo_model(pybamm.InputParameter("period"))
        # The same solver resets wherever each input set's period puts it
        for value, result in [(period, expected), (5.0, 50.0)]:
            solution = solver.solve(model, [0, 20], inputs={"period": value})
            np.testing.assert_allclose(solution["v"](20), result, rtol=1e-6)

7    def test_step_does_not_reuse_other_input_sensitivities(self):
        model = pybamm.BaseModel()
        u = pybamm.Variable("u")
        a = pybamm.InputParameter("a")
        b = pybamm.InputParameter("b")
        model.rhs = {u: a + 2 * b}
        model.initial_conditions = {u: 0}
        model.variables = {"u": u}
        inputs = {"a": 1.0, "b": 1.0}
        solution = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver().step(
            None, model, 1, inputs=inputs, calculate_sensitivities=["a"]
        )
        with pytest.raises(pybamm.SolverError, match=r"\['b'\]"):
            pybamm.IDAKLUSolver().step(
                solution, model, 1, inputs=inputs, calculate_sensitivities=["b"]
            )
        solution = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver().step(
            None, model, 1, inputs=inputs, calculate_sensitivities=["b", "a"]
        )
        solution = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver().step(
            solution, model, 1, inputs=inputs, calculate_sensitivities=["b"]
        )
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            solution["u"].sensitivities["b"][-1], 4, rtol=1e-10
        )
    def test_step_changing_calculate_sensitivities(self):
        model = pybamm.BaseModel()
        u = pybamm.Variable("u")
        model.rhs = {u: pybamm.InputParameter("a")}
        model.initial_conditions = {u: 0}
        model.variables = {"u": u}
        solver = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver()
        inputs = {"a": 2.0}
        solution = solver.step(None, model, 1, inputs=inputs, 
calculate_sensitivities=True)
        solution = solver.step(solution, model, 1, inputs=inputs)
        np.testing.assert_allclose(solution["u"](2), 4, rtol=1e-10)
        solution = solver.step(None, model, 1, inputs=inputs, 
calculate_sensitivities=True)
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            solution["u"].sensitivities["a"][-1], 1, rtol=1e-10
        )
    @pytest.mark.parametrize("use_mapper", [False, True])
    def test_step_sensitivities_across_change_of_model(self, use_mapper):
        k, scale, reference = (pybamm.InputParameter(n) for n in "ksr")
        def model_with(new_model):
            model = pybamm.BaseModel()
            u = pybamm.Variable(
                "u",
                scale=scale if new_model else 1,
                reference=reference if new_model else 0,
            )
            z = pybamm.Variable("z")
            model.rhs = {u: -k * u, z: scale + reference}
            model.initial_conditions = {u: 1.0, z: 0.0}
            model.variables = {"u": u, "z": z}
            if new_model:
                w = pybamm.Variable("w")
                model.rhs[w] = pybamm.Scalar(0)
                model.initial_conditions[w] = 0.0
                model.variables["w"] = w
            else:
                # w starts the new model from a value depending on k directly
                model.variables["w"] = k * u
            pybamm.Discretisation().process_model(model)
            return model
        previous_model, model = model_with(False), model_with(True)
        inputs = {"k": 0.7, "s": 3.0, "r": 0.5}
        names = ["k", "r", "s"]
        solution = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(rtol=1e-10, atol=1e-12).step(
            None, previous_model, 1.0, inputs=inputs, 
calculate_sensitivities=names
        )
        state_mapper = None
        if use_mapper:
            mapper = model.build_initial_state_mapper(previous_model)
            vars_for_processing = pybamm.BaseSolver._get_vars_for_processing(
                previous_model, inputs
            )
            state_mapper = pybamm.solvers.base_solver.process(
                mapper, "mapper", vars_for_processing
            )[:3]
        solution = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(rtol=1e-10, atol=1e-12).step(
            solution,
            model,
            1.0,
            inputs=inputs,
            calculate_sensitivities=names,
            state_mapper=state_mapper,
        )
        t = 2.0
        u_sensitivities = solution["u"].sensitivities
        np.testing.assert_allclose(solution["u"](t), np.exp(-0.7 * t), 
rtol=1e-8)
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            u_sensitivities["k"][-1], -t * np.exp(-0.7 * t), rtol=1e-7
        )
        np.testing.assert_allclose(u_sensitivities["r"][-1], 0, atol=1e-10)
        np.testing.assert_allclose(u_sensitivities["s"][-1], 0, atol=1e-10)
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            solution["w"].sensitivities["k"][-1], 0.3 * np.exp(-0.7), rtol=1e-7
        )

Reasoning:
I'm checking the mapper test now: the value comes out as -7.2, so this points at
the y0 value path through the mapper rather than at sensitivity. The likely 
cause is input ordering, since my test passes the inputs as k, s, r while the 
step stacks them sorted as k, r, s, and Simulation sorts the names on its side. 
In r8 I had used s.

The fix is to sort the inputs in the test so they match the order the step uses,
and the `base_solver.process` call in the test is fine as it is.

    @pytest.mark.parametrize(
        "solver_cls",
        [pybamm.lithium_ion.SPM, pybamm.lithium_ion.DFN],
        ids=["SPM", "DFN"],
    )
    def test_solve_with_sensitivities_and_experiment(self, solver_cls):
        experiment_2step = pybamm.Experiment(
            [
                (
                    "Discharge at C/20 for 2 min",
                    "Charge at 1 A for 1 min",
                    "Hold at 4.1 V for 1 min",
                    "Discharge at 2 W for 1 min",
                    "Discharge at 2 W for 1 min",  # repeat to cover this case 
(changes initialisation)
                ),
            ]
            * 2,
        )

        solutions = []
        input_param_name = "Negative electrode active material volume fraction"
        solver = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(atol=1e-8, rtol=1e-8)
        for calculate_sensitivities in [False, True]:
            model = solver_cls()
            param = model.default_parameter_values
            input_param_value = param[input_param_name]
            param.update({input_param_name: "[input]"})
            sim = pybamm.Simulation(
                model,
                experiment=experiment_2step,
                solver=solver,
                parameter_values=param,
            )
            solution = sim.solve(
                inputs={input_param_name: input_param_value},
                calculate_sensitivities=calculate_sensitivities,
            )
            solutions.append(solution)

        model = solver_cls()
        param = model.default_parameter_values
        base_input_param_value = param[input_param_name]
        fd_tol = 1e-4
        for dh in [-fd_tol, fd_tol]:
            model = solver_cls()
            param = model.default_parameter_values
            input_param_value = base_input_param_value * (1.0 + dh)
            param.update({input_param_name: "[input]"})
            sim = pybamm.Simulation(
                model,
                experiment=experiment_2step,
                solver=solver,
                parameter_values=param,
            )
            solution = sim.solve(
                inputs={input_param_name: input_param_value},
            )
            solutions.append(solution)

        # check solutions are the same
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            solutions[0]["Voltage [V]"].data,
            solutions[1]["Voltage [V]"](solutions[0].t),
            rtol=5e-2,
            equal_nan=True,
        )
        sensitivity_keys = set(solutions[1]["Voltage [V]"].sensitivities)
        assert input_param_name in sensitivity_keys
        assert pybamm.Simulation._STEP_INDEX_INPUT not in sensitivity_keys

        # use finite difference to check sensitivities
        t = solutions[0].t
        soln_neg = solutions[2]["Voltage [V]"](t)
        soln_pos = solutions[3]["Voltage [V]"](t)
        sens_fd = (soln_pos - soln_neg) / (2 * fd_tol * base_input_param_value)
        sens_idaklu = np.interp(
            t,
            solutions[1].t,
            solutions[1]["Voltage 
[V]"].sensitivities[input_param_name].flatten(),
        )
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            sens_fd,
            sens_idaklu,
            rtol=2e-4,
            atol=2e-3,
        )

$ cat >> /app/pybamm/packages/pybamm/tests/unit/test_solvers/test_base_solver.py
<<'EOF'

    @staticmethod
    def _set_up_input_switch_model(solver, t_eval):
        model = pybamm.BaseModel()
        v = pybamm.Variable("v")
        model.rhs = {v: pybamm.t > pybamm.InputParameter("t_switch")}
        model.initial_conditions = {v: 0}
        model.variables = {"v": v}
        pybamm.Discretisation().process_model(model)
        solver.set_up(model, {"t_switch": 4.0}, t_eval)
        return model

    @pytest.mark.parametrize(
        "t_eval",
        [np.array([0, 10]), np.arange(11), np.linspace(0, 10, 11)],
        ids=["int-ends", "int-points", "float-points"],
    )
    def test_discontinuity_indices_leave_t_eval_unchanged(self, t_eval):
        solver = pybamm.BaseSolver()
        model = self._set_up_input_switch_model(solver, t_eval)
        t_eval_before = t_eval.copy()

        start_indices, end_indices, new_t_eval = (
            solver._get_discontinuity_start_end_indices(
                model, model.y0_list[0], {"t_switch": 4.0}, t_eval
            )
        )

        np.testing.assert_array_equal(t_eval, t_eval_before)
        eps = np.finfo(float).eps
        assert len(start_indices) == len(end_indices) == 2
        assert new_t_eval[end_indices[0] - 1] == 4 * (1 - eps)
        assert new_t_eval[start_indices[1]] == 4 * (1 + eps)
        assert end_indices[-1] == len(new_t_eval)
        assert np.all(np.diff(new_t_eval) > 0)

    def test_discontinuity_within_a_float_of_t_eval_ends_is_not_split(self):
        solver = pybamm.BaseSolver()
        model = self._set_up_input_switch_model(solver, [0, 1])
        for t_eval in [
            np.array([np.nextafter(4.0, -np.inf), 10.0]),
            np.array([0.0, np.nextafter(4.0, np.inf)]),
        ]:
            assert solver._get_discontinuity_start_end_indices(
                model, model.y0_list[0], {"t_switch": 4.0}, t_eval
            ) == ([0], [2], pytest.approx(t_eval, rel=0))

    def test_discontinuity_events_are_added_once(self):
        model = pybamm.BaseModel()
        v = pybamm.Variable("v")
        switch = pybamm.t > pybamm.InputParameter("t_switch")
        model.rhs = {v: switch + 2 * switch}
        model.initial_conditions = {v: 0}
        model.variables = {"v": v}
        pybamm.Discretisation().process_model(model)

        solver = pybamm.BaseSolver()
        for _ in range(3):
            solver.set_up(model, {"t_switch": 4.0}, [0, 10])
        discontinuities = [
            event
            for event in model.events
            if event.event_type == pybamm.EventType.DISCONTINUITY
        ]
        assert len(discontinuities) == 1

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("method", ["solve", "step"])
    def test_second_model_error_leaves_calculate_sensitivities(self, method):
        def make_model():
            model = pybamm.BaseModel()
            u = pybamm.Variable("u")
            model.rhs = {u: pybamm.InputParameter("a")}
            model.initial_conditions = {u: 0}
            model.variables = {"u": u}
            return model

        solver = pybamm.IDAKLUSolver()
        solver.solve(make_model(), [0, 1], inputs={"a": 1.0})
        model = make_model()
        model.calculate_sensitivities = []
        with pytest.raises(RuntimeError, match="already been initialised"):
            if method == "solve":
                solver.solve(
                    model, [0, 1], inputs={"a": 1.0}, 
calculate_sensitivities=True
                )
            else:
                solver.step(
                    None, model, 1, inputs={"a": 1.0}, 
calculate_sensitivities=True
                )
        assert model.calculate_sensitivities == []
EOF

$ cd /app/pybamm/packages/pybamm && python - <<'EOF'
p = "tests/unit/test_experiments/test_simulation_with_experiment.py"
s = open(p).read()
anchor = "    def 
test_solve_with_sensitivity_list_ignores_internal_experiment_input(self):\n"
new = '''    @pytest.mark.parametrize("mode", ["legacy", "unified"])
    def test_sensitivities_across_steps_with_input_scale(self, mode):
        # The maximum concentration scales the particle concentrations
        name = "Maximum concentration in negative electrode [mol.m-3]"
        value = 33133.0
        experiment = pybamm.Experiment(
            [
                "Discharge at 1C for 5 minutes",
                "Hold at 3.6 V for 2 minutes",
                "Discharge at 0.5C for 2 minutes",
            ]
        )

        def solve(inputs, **kwargs):
            parameter_values = pybamm.ParameterValues("Chen2020")
            parameter_values.update({name: "[input]"})
            sim = pybamm.Simulation(
                pybamm.lithium_ion.SPM(),
                experiment=experiment,
                parameter_values=parameter_values,
                solver=pybamm.IDAKLUSolver(rtol=1e-8, atol=1e-10),
                experiment_model_mode=mode,
            )
            return sim, sim.solve(inputs=inputs, **kwargs)

        sim, solution = solve({name: value}, calculate_sensitivities=True)
        sensitivity = solution["Voltage [V]"].sensitivities[name][-1].item()

        step = value * 1e-4
        voltages = [
            solve({name: value + sign * step})[1]["Voltage [V]"].entries[-1]
            for sign in [-1, 1]
        ]
        finite_difference = (voltages[1] - voltages[0]) / (2 * step)
        np.testing.assert_allclose(sensitivity, finite_difference, rtol=1e-3)

        # The same simulation solves again without sensitivities
        repeat = sim.solve(inputs={name: value})
        np.testing.assert_allclose(
            repeat["Voltage [V]"].entries[-1],
            solution["Voltage [V]"].entries[-1],
            rtol=1e-6,
        )

'''
assert anchor in s
s = s.replace(anchor, new + anchor, 1)
open(p, "w").write(s)
EOF

default casadi-format models but not for jax-format ones. I'm also noting that 
the extra jacobian evaluation on each step should be cheap because casadi caches
it, and that non-sensitivity runs should be unaffected.

(content before edit)
  3|## Bug fixes
  4|
  5|- Step and experiment times given as strings, such as `"Rest for -5 
minutes"` or `"0 seconds"`, now raise `ValueError: time must be positive`, as 
numeric times already did. Before, they gave a negative or zero duration or 
period. Step temperatures can now be NumPy scalars (e.g. `np.int64(298)`), which
used to raise an `AttributeError`. 
([#5920](https://github.com/pybamm-team/PyBaMM/issues/5920), 
[#5923](https://github.com/pybamm-team/PyBaMM/pull/5923))
  6|- `IDAKLUSolver.jaxify` now returns correct derivatives at any requested 
time. Before, it dropped negative tangents and scalar-time cotangents, and read 
sensitivities from the wrong solver step, or from the nearest `t_eval` point at 
off-grid times. ([#5916](https://github.com/pybamm-team/PyBaMM/pull/5916))
(content after edit)
  3|## Bug fixes
  4|
+ 5|- Solver restarts now continue from the right state and sensitivities.
+ 6|  - `solve` restarts each input set at a discontinuity, such as `pybamm.t > 
pybamm.InputParameter("t_switch")`, from the state and sensitivities it reached 
there, algebraic states included, with or without `calc_ic`. Before, 
`IDAKLUSolver` restarted from the initial conditions, and a DAE could fail with 
`IDA_BAD_K`. An input set that stops on an event keeps its own termination, and 
integer `t_eval`, discontinuities on `t_eval` points, `t_interp` and 
`store_first_last=True` work. `t_eval` is no longer modified.
+ 7|  - `step` stops at discontinuities and `Modulo` resets, as `solve` does. 
`pybamm.Modulo(pybamm.t, period)` takes an input or infinite period, and rejects
a time-dependent one with a `SolverError`.

a `starting_solution`, are the derivatives of the new model's states, including 
with an input-dependent scale or reference. Before, they could be orders of 
magnitude off, or raise an `AttributeError`.

`SolverError` naming any input the solution has none for. Changing 
`calculate_sensitivities` between `step` calls no longer raises a 
`RuntimeError`, so an experiment can be re-solved without sensitivities.

leaves `model.calculate_sensitivities` unchanged.

numeric times already did. Before, they gave a negative or zero duration or

used to raise an `AttributeError`.

Summary: finish: {"message": "All seven restart problems are fixed in 
`solvers/base_solver.py` and `solvers/idaklu_solver.py`. The existing solver, 
simulation and experiment tests pass, except for ones that fail on the original 
code too: those that download data (no network here), a LaTeX preview test, and 
a few that crash or run out of memory when run in parallel. The VTK plotting 
tests segfault in this headless environment.\n\n**What was wrong, briefly:** 
IDAKLU starts from `model.y0full`, which was never updated between segments, so 
every later segment restarted from the initial conditions. The segment loop also
only checked whether the first input set had stopped. `step()` ignored 
discontinuities completely. Sensitivities carried to a new model did `(sens - 
reference) / scale.value`: subtracting the reference from a derivative gives the
wrong answer, and `.value` doesn't exist on an input-dependent 
scale.\n\n**Changes:**\n1. **Restarts in `solve()`:** both `solve()` and 
`step()` now go through a shared segment loop. Each input set restarts from its 
own final state and sensitivities, and stops when it hits an event; the others 
carry on. `t_interp` is cut to each segment, and the model's initial states are 
put back afterwards.\n2. **Splitting `t_eval`:** 
`_get_discontinuity_start_end_indices` keeps its signature and return value. It 
now works on a float copy, so the caller's `t_eval` is untouched and integer 
arrays work. A discontinuity within a float of a segment boundary is no longer 
split on, so `t_eval` can't come out out of order.\n3. **`step()` and 
`Modulo`:** `step()` now stops at discontinuities, including `Modulo` resets. 
Reset times are worked out from the window being integrated and each input set's
period. An infinite period never resets, and a period that depends on time (or 
on the states) raises a `SolverError`.\n4. **Duplicate events:** a discontinuity
event is only added to `model.events` if it isn't there already.\n5. **Change of
model:** sensitivities use the chain rule through the state map (`dy0/dp = df/dy
\u00b7 dy/dp + df/dp`), with or without a `state_mapper`. This covers 
input-dependent scales and references, and states that start from an input 
directly.\n6. **Continuing `step()`:** sensitivities are matched by input name. 
If the solution being continued has none for a requested input, you get a 
`SolverError` listing them, e.g. `['b']`.\n7. **`calculate_sensitivities`:** 
changing it between `step()` calls re-sets-up the model like `solve()` does. The
model's value is only written after the \"already initialised for another 
model\" check, so that `RuntimeError` leaves it unchanged.\n\n**One addition you
didn't ask for:** with `calc_ic=False`, the algebraic sensitivities at a restart
are now recomputed from the algebraic equations. Without this, the stored 
sensitivity at the restart point was stale (1 instead of 5 in my test), even 
though later points were right.\n\n**Checks:** finite differences confirm the 
experiment sensitivities in both `\"legacy\"` and `\"unified\"` modes, including
with an input maximum concentration, which used to raise `AttributeError`. 
Results with no discontinuities or sensitivities match the original code 
exactly, at the same speed.\n\n**Two things to know:**\n- Sensitivities with 
respect to a switch time itself (e.g. `d/d t_switch`) are still zero. That's the
limitation the docstrings already mention, and I didn't change it.\n- Input sets
whose discontinuities fall at different times still raise \"Discontinuity events
occur at different times\", as the existing test expects.\n\nI also added 45 
tests (in `test_idaklu_solver.py`, `test_base_solver.py` and 
`test_simulation_with_experiment.py`) and a CHANGELOG bullet, which has no PR 
link because there's no PR number yet. These are outside `src/pybamm`, so 
they'll be discarded along with any other changes outside it. To run the tests 
here I had to put stand-ins for the missing `hypothesis` and `pytest-mock` 
packages in `/tmp`, outside the repository."}

Finish with message:
All seven restart problems are fixed in `solvers/base_solver.py` and 
`solvers/idaklu_solver.py`. The existing solver, simulation and experiment tests
pass, except for ones that fail on the original code too: those that download 
data (no network here), a LaTeX preview test, and a few that crash or run out of
memory when run in parallel. The VTK plotting tests segfault in this headless 
environment.

**What was wrong, briefly:** IDAKLU starts from `model.y0full`, which was never 
updated between segments, so every later segment restarted from the initial 
conditions. The segment loop also only checked whether the first input set had 
stopped. `step()` ignored discontinuities completely. Sensitivities carried to a
new model did `(sens - reference) / scale.value`: subtracting the reference from
a derivative gives the wrong answer, and `.value` doesn't exist on an 
input-dependent scale.

**Changes:**
1. **Restarts in `solve()`:** both `solve()` and `step()` now go through a 
shared segment loop. Each input set restarts from its own final state and 
sensitivities, and stops when it hits an event; the others carry on. `t_interp` 
is cut to each segment, and the model's initial states are put back afterwards.
2. **Splitting `t_eval`:** `_get_discontinuity_start_end_indices` keeps its 
signature and return value. It now works on a float copy, so the caller's 
`t_eval` is untouched and integer arrays work. A discontinuity within a float of
a segment boundary is no longer split on, so `t_eval` can't come out out of 
order.
3. **`step()` and `Modulo`:** `step()` now stops at discontinuities, including 
`Modulo` resets. Reset times are worked out from the window being integrated and
each input set's period. An infinite period never resets, and a period that 
depends on time (or on the states) raises a `SolverError`.
4. **Duplicate events:** a discontinuity event is only added to `model.events` 
if it isn't there already.
5. **Change of model:** sensitivities use the chain rule through the state map 
(`dy0/dp = df/dy · dy/dp + df/dp`), with or without a `state_mapper`. This 
covers input-dependent scales and references, and states that start from an 
input directly.
6. **Continuing `step()`:** sensitivities are matched by input name. If the 
solution being continued has none for a requested input, you get a `SolverError`
listing them, e.g. `['b']`.
7. **`calculate_sensitivities`:** changing it between `step()` calls re-sets-up 
the model like `solve()` does. The model's value is only written after the 
"already initialised for another model" check, so that `RuntimeError` leaves it 
unchanged.

**One addition you didn't ask for:** with `calc_ic=False`, the algebraic 
sensitivities at a restart are now recomputed from the algebraic equations. 
Without this, the stored sensitivity at the restart point was stale (1 instead 
of 5 in my test), even though later points were right.

**Checks:** finite differences confirm the experiment sensitivities in both 
`"legacy"` and `"unified"` modes, including with an input maximum concentration,
which used to raise `AttributeError`. Results with no discontinuities or 
sensitivities match the original code exactly, at the same speed.

**Two things to know:**
- Sensitivities with respect to a switch time itself (e.g. `d/d t_switch`) are 
still zero. That's the limitation the docstrings already mention, and I didn't 
change it.
- Input sets whose discontinuities fall at different times still raise 
"Discontinuity events occur at different times", as the existing test expects.

I also added 45 tests (in `test_idaklu_solver.py`, `test_base_solver.py` and 
`test_simulation_with_experiment.py`) and a CHANGELOG bullet, which has no PR 
link because there's no PR number yet. These are outside `src/pybamm`, so 
they'll be discarded along with any other changes outside it. To run the tests 
here I had to put stand-ins for the missing `hypothesis` and `pytest-mock` 
packages in `/tmp`, outside the repository.
